`transition(to, reason)` throws on an illegal edge. Every transition writes a timeline row, a `setState` update, an index upsert, and a NotifyWorkflow for watch incidents.

## 11. Safety implementation

- **SSRF (`safety/ssrf.ts`, `iprange.ts`):**
  - Only `http` and `https` schemes, and only port 80/443 (an empty port is allowed).
  - Reject IP literals, `localhost`/`*.localhost`/`.local`/`.internal`, single-label hosts and URLs with credentials.
  - Resolve the host with DoH before fetching and reject it if **any** A or AAAA answer is in a blocked range: 0/8, 10/8, 100.64/10, 127/8, 169.254/16, 172.16/12, 192.0.0/24, 192.0.2/24, 192.168/16, 198.18/15, 198.51.100/24, 203.0.113/24, 224/4, 240/4, ::/128, ::1, fc00::/7, fe80::/10, ff00::/8, ::ffff:0:0/96 (checked against the embedded v4 address), 64:ff9b::/96, 2001:db8::/32. WP-04 also blocks ::/96 (IPv4-compatible), 100::/64, and 6to4 `2002::/16` checked against its embedded v4 address. Anything that doesn't parse is blocked.
  - Apply the same check to every redirect hop.
  - **Dev-only exception:** when `SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN` is set, that one exact origin (scheme, host and port, for example the local demo service) skips the checks above. It's set only in `.dev.vars` for local work and never in `wrangler.jsonc` `vars`, so in production the demo is reached through its public `workers.dev` URL and everything private stays blocked. There's no wildcard and no partial match.
  - Defense in depth: `global_fetch_strictly_public`. A DNS-rebinding race can't be fully closed, and the README says so.
- **Open proxy:** GET/HEAD only. No headers, bodies or cookies from the user. `reader.ts` caps reads at 16 KB, aborts after 10 s, and never follows redirects automatically. Origin pin (D6).
- **Probe rate:** `PROBE_LIMIT.limit({key: hostname})` runs before every probe sample. If it's exceeded, the probe returns `unavailable: rate_limited`.
- **Quotas (D14):** `RUN_LIMIT.limit({key: ws})` plus the D1 fixed-window `usage_counters(kind='run')`, which uses an atomic `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET count = count + 1 RETURNING count`.
- **Budget guard:** before every run, `llm_usage(today).neurons_est ≥ 9000` → `mode = rule_based`. `GET /api/session` returns `budgetMode`, which drives the `BudgetBanner`.
- **Webhooks:**
  - Only https URLs on `hooks.slack.com`, or `discord.com`/`discordapp.com` with a path starting `/api/webhooks/`.
  - Encrypted with AES-GCM-256 using the `WEBHOOK_KEY` secret, a 12-byte random IV, and AAD = workspaceId.
  - `url_hint` is stored masked (`hooks.slack.com/…/••••abcd`).
  - The URL is never sent to the LLM or written to logs.
  - Payload: `{text}` for Slack, `{content}` for Discord. It contains the summary, status and incident link.
- **Prompt injection:** `<untrusted>` wrapping, snippet truncation, zod plus origin-pin validation, the fixed action catalog, and human approval for every action.
- **Accepted risks:** D2 (unprotected for about a day), D7 (workspace ID in the demo URL), probes from a single location (R5).

## 12. Frontend

### 12.1 Layout (`AppShell`)

- **Header:** wordmark "Incident Copilot", `BudgetBanner`, and a GitHub link. Don't use the Cloudflare logo; "Built on Cloudflare" in the footer is enough.
- **Left `Sidebar`:** "New incident" and the incident list (status badge, unread dot, polled every 10 s), followed by `WatchlistPanel` and `WebhookSettings`.
- **Main area:** `ChatPane`.
- **Right column:** `DemoPanel`. Below the `lg` breakpoint it becomes a closed "Demo service" disclosure at the top of `<main>` (an `aria-expanded` button, as for evidence). `useMediaQuery` mounts it in one place only, so the panel keeps a single state.

### 12.2 Components

- **`ChatPane`:** messages, check cards inline in run order, approval cards, and a composer that's disabled while `activeRunId` is set. The empty state shows chips for "Use demo service" and "diagnose cloudflare.com". The composer includes `VoiceButton`.
----
Incident, workspace and approval IDs are 128-bit random hex values (`crypto.getRandomValues`). Evidence IDs are `<tool>-<n>`, with prefixes `dns`, `http`, `cert` and `status`, and n is unique per incident. Workflow IDs are `diag-<incidentId>-<seq>`, `watch-<watchId>-<epochMinute>` and `notify-<incidentId>-<status>`.

## 7. Interfaces

### 7.1 HTTP API (Hono, all paths need the workspace cookie, which is created on first request)

| Method and path | Body / query | Response | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/session` | | `{demoUrl, budgetMode, limits}` | Sets `__Host-ws` (HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax, 1 year) if it's missing |
| `GET /api/incidents` | | `[{id,target,status,origin,unread,updatedAt}]` | Sidebar polling (D16) |
| `POST /api/incidents` | `{message}` | `{id}` / 400 `{error:"need_target"}` / 429 `{retryAfter}` / 503 `{error:"demo_unavailable"}` | Target parse (D9), then quota (D14), then `IncidentAgent.init`. 503 while `DEMO_BASE_URL` still holds the `<sub>` placeholder |
| `GET /api/incidents/:id` | | Snapshot (`IncidentState` + findings) | Owner only. Used by eval and smoke tests. |
| `POST /api/incidents/:id/read` | | 204 | Clears `unread` |
| `GET /api/demo` · `POST /api/demo/mode` | `{modes}` | `{modes, url}` / 400 `{error:"invalid_modes"}` / 503 `{error:"demo_unavailable"}` | Calls `env.DEMO.getModes` / `setModes(ws, modes)`. `modes` is `["healthy"]` or one problem plus an optional `"slow"` (§7.5). 503 while `DEMO_BASE_URL` holds `<sub>` |
| `GET/POST /api/watches` · `DELETE /api/watches/:id` | `{url, intervalSec}` | Watch list | Per-workspace and global caps |
| `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/webhook` | `{url}` | `{kind, hint}` | Allowlist, then encryption |
| `POST /api/voice` | `audio/webm` body | `{text}` | Size cap, then limit, then whisper, then normalization |
| `GET /api/health` | | `{ok, version}` | Smoke test |

### 7.2 IncidentAgent: RPC (Worker and Workflow) and callables (client)

```ts
// RPC (server-side only)
init(p: { workspaceId; target; origin: "chat"|"watch"; message?: string; watchId?: string }): Promise<void>
getRunContext(runId): Promise<RunContext>          // prior evidence digests, convo tail, budget mode
addPendingChecks(runId, calls: PlannedCall[]): Promise<string[]>   // allocates evidence IDs
recordCheck(evidenceId, result: ToolResult): Promise<void>         // upsert → idempotent
setFindings(runId, f: Findings): Promise<void>
narrate(runId): Promise<{ messageId: string }>                    // streams; idempotent per runId (D4)
proposeAction(runId, workflowId): Promise<{ approvalId: string | null; actionId }>   // picks the action itself (§7.6)
recordApprovalOutcome(approvalId, "rejected"|"expired"|"failed", detail?): Promise<void>
executeAction(approvalId): Promise<{ ok, text }>                   // WP-12: idempotent
afterAction(runId, approvalId): Promise<{ pass, text }>            // WP-12: runs quickCheck itself → mitigated + monitor
monitorTick(): Promise<void>                                       // WP-12: agent schedule callback (also RPC for tests)
reportWatchCheck(pass: boolean, digest): Promise<void>             // from cron
// @callable (WebSocket). Every method except getEvidence requires role=owner (D8)
sendMessage(text) · approve(approvalId) · reject(approvalId) · resolve() · generatePostmortem()
markRead() · getEvidence(evidenceId)
```

`onConnect(conn, ctx)` parses the cookie from `ctx.request` and calls `conn.setState({ role })`. Owner-only callables use `getCurrentAgent().connection.state.role`.

*WP-08 as built:*
- **Signatures:** `addPendingChecks(runId, turn, calls)`; `setFindings(runId, findings, mode)`; `finishRun(runId, status, {toolCalls, llmTurns})` (first call wins); `startRun(question, kind)` throws `run_active`; plus `getSnapshot()`.
- **Viewers:** `shouldConnectionBeReadonly` also marks them readonly. A socket for an incident that doesn't exist is closed with 4404.
- **Routing:** the Worker only routes WebSocket upgrades to `/agents/incident-agent/<32-hex id>`. Every other agent path and plain HTTP gets a 404, so no stray Durable Objects are created.
- **Callables without decorator syntax:** `@callable()` only records the method in the SDK's registry (the decorator context is unused), so `IncidentAgent.ts` registers `sendMessage`, `resolve`, `markRead` and `getEvidence` with `callable()(fn)` after the class. That avoids the Babel decorator transform and the ES2021 target the SDK otherwise needs in Vite and Vitest.
- **Typed stub:** `agent/stub.ts` `incidentAgent(env, id)` wraps `getAgentByName`, whose type only accepts agents with `unknown` state.
- **Deferred to their WPs:** the Workflow launch (WP-09, marked in `startRun`); `proposeAction`, `recordApprovalOutcome`, `afterAction`, approve/reject and the monitor (WP-12); quotas (WP-17); `reportWatchCheck` (WP-18); `generatePostmortem` (WP-22).

### 7.3 Tool contracts (`tools/registry.ts`). Inputs use zod. Outputs are `ToolResult {evidenceId, ok, status, summary, digest, raw}`.

| Tool | Input | Digest (what the LLM sees, ≤ 1.5 KB) | Max subrequests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dns_lookup` | `{hostname, types?: ("A"\|"AAAA"\|"CNAME"\|"NS"\|"MX"\|"TXT")[] = ["A","AAAA"]}` | For each resolver (1.1.1.1 `cloudflare-dns.com/dns-query`, 8.8.8.8 `dns.google/resolve`, both DoH JSON): status, answers and TTLs, the CNAME chain, a `mismatch` flag (the resolvers disagree on the response code or on whether the name has any address), `addressesDiffer` (different address sets; informational, normal for geo/CDN DNS), and `ssrfBlocked` if any answer is private | 2 × types |
| `http_probe` | `{url, method?: "GET"\|"HEAD" = "GET"}` | For each of 3 samples: status, TTFB and total ms. Also the hop list (≤ 5, `redirect: "manual"`, each hop SSRF-checked), selected headers (`server`, `cf-ray`, `cf-cache-status`, `cache-control`, `content-type`, `location`, `set-cookie` as present or absent), a `<untrusted>` snippet of ≤ 500 chars, and an `asset` sub-probe (D10) with its URL, status, `cache-control` and whether `set-cookie` was present | Capped at **20** with `countingFetch` (2 guard DoH lookups + 3 × (1 + 5)). The asset sub-probe and extra hosts in a redirect chain only run if room is left, and samples 2–3 are skipped after a loop, hop cap or refusal. Hitting the cap gives a partial result (`partial: "budget"`), not an error. |

-- TDD §6.1: the full D1 schema from day one.
CREATE TABLE workspaces (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL);
CREATE TABLE incidents_index (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL, target TEXT NOT NULL,
  status TEXT NOT NULL, origin TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'chat',          -- chat | watch
  unread INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL, updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL);
CREATE INDEX idx_incidents_ws ON incidents_index(workspace_id, updated_at DESC);
CREATE TABLE watches (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL, url TEXT NOT NULL,
  interval_sec INTEGER NOT NULL, next_run INTEGER NOT NULL, expires_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
  last_status TEXT, consecutive_passes INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, open_incident_id TEXT);
CREATE INDEX idx_watches_due ON watches(next_run);
CREATE TABLE webhooks (workspace_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, kind TEXT NOT NULL, url_ciphertext BLOB NOT NULL,
  iv BLOB NOT NULL, url_hint TEXT NOT NULL, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL);   -- url_hint = masked display
CREATE TABLE llm_usage (day TEXT PRIMARY KEY, neurons_est INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0);
CREATE TABLE usage_counters (workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL, kind TEXT NOT NULL, window_start INTEGER NOT NULL,
  count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, PRIMARY KEY (workspace_id, kind, window_start));  -- kind: run | voice
````

    10	/** `/agents/incident-agent/<32-hex id>` only: other names would create empty Durable Objects. */
    11	function isIncidentPath(request: Request): boolean {
    12	  const [, prefix, agent, name, ...rest] = new URL(request.url).pathname.split("/");
    13	  return prefix === "agents" && agent === "incident-agent" && isId(name) && rest.length === 0;
    14	}
    15	
    16	/**
    17	 * True only when `DEMO_BASE_URL` is this Worker's own origin, i.e. local dev: under Vite the demo Worker has no
    18	 * port of its own, so `/w/*` is passed through the DEMO binding. Deployed, the demo has its own workers.dev URL
    19	 * and `/w/*` here is a 404 (TDD §7.5).
    20	 */
    21	function isLocalDemo(url: URL, env: Env): boolean {
    22	  return URL.canParse(env.DEMO_BASE_URL) && new URL(env.DEMO_BASE_URL).origin === url.origin;
    23	}
    24	
    25	// Only /api/*, /agents/* and /w/* reach the Worker (run_worker_first); everything else is served from assets.
    26	export default {
    27	  async fetch(request, env, ctx) {
    28	    const url = new URL(request.url);
    29	    if (url.pathname.startsWith("/w/")) return isLocalDemo(url, env) ? env.DEMO.fetch(request) : notFound();
    30	    if (url.pathname.startsWith("/agents/")) {
    31	      // WebSockets only: the app never talks to the agent over plain HTTP.
    32	      const routed = await routeAgentRequest(request, env, {
    33	        onBeforeConnect: (req) => (isIncidentPath(req) ? undefined : notFound()),
    34	        onBeforeRequest: () => notFound(),
    35	      });
    36	      return routed ?? notFound();
    37	    }
    38	    return api.fetch(request, env, ctx);
    39	  },
    40	} satisfies ExportedHandler<Env>;

import type { Fetch } from "../lib/doh";

/** Run bounds (TDD §5.2). */
export const MAX_TOOL_CALLS = 6;
export const MAX_PLAN_TURNS = 3;
export const RUN_DEADLINE_MS = 60_000;
export const SUBREQUEST_BUDGET = 45;
export const HTTP_SAMPLES = 3;
export const SNIPPET_TO_LLM = 500;
export const EVIDENCE_DIGEST_MAX = 1536;
export const HIGH_LATENCY_TOTAL_MS = 2000;
export const HIGH_LATENCY_TTFB_MS = 1500;
/** Per LLM call. gpt once took 19 s on a forced submit_findings turn; a timeout moves to the fast fallback. */
export const LLM_TIMEOUT_MS = 25_000;
/** Workers AI free allocation per day, and where the budget guard switches to rule-based mode (WP-17). */
export const NEURON_DAILY = 10_000;
export const BUDGET_GUARD_AT = 9_000;

export class BudgetExceededError extends Error {
  override name = "BudgetExceededError";
}

/**
 * Per-run subrequest budget. Serialisable so it can live in the run row between Workflow steps.
 * A tool reserves its worst case up front, then settles with what it actually used.
 */
export class SubrequestBudget {
  constructor(
    readonly limit: number = SUBREQUEST_BUDGET,
    private used = 0,
    private reserved = 0,
  ) {}

  static from(json: { limit: number; used: number; reserved?: number }): SubrequestBudget {
    return new SubrequestBudget(json.limit, json.used, json.reserved ?? 0);
  }

  toJSON() {
    return { limit: this.limit, used: this.used, reserved: this.reserved };
  }

  get spent(): number {
    return this.used;
  }

  remaining(): number {
    return this.limit - this.used - this.reserved;
  }

  reserve(n: number): boolean {
    if (n > this.remaining()) return false;
    this.reserved += n;
    return true;
  }

  settle(reserved: number, actual: number): void {
    this.reserved = Math.max(0, this.reserved - reserved);
    this.used += actual;
  }
}

/** Counts real outbound requests against a hard cap; used inside a tool call and by cron. */
export class SubrequestCounter {
  count = 0;
  constructor(readonly limit: number) {}
}

  /** Server-side RPC has no connection and is trusted; WebSocket callers must be the owner. */
  private requireOwner() {

  // ---- RPC: Worker and Workflow (TDD §7.2) ----

  /** Idempotent: a retried create (Worker or WatchWorkflow step) returns the existing incident's run. */
  async init(p: InitParams): Promise<{ runId: string | null }> {
    if (this.incident()) return { runId: this.activeRunId() };
    const now = Date.now();
    this.sql`INSERT INTO incident (id, workspace_id, target, origin, status, watch_id, created_at, updated_at)
      VALUES (${this.name}, ${p.workspaceId}, ${p.target}, ${p.origin}, 'investigating', ${p.watchId ?? null}, ${now}, ${now})`;
    this.event("opened", { origin: p.origin, target: p.target });
    const question = p.message ?? `Scheduled check failed for ${p.target}`;
    const runId = await this.startRun(question, "initial", p.message ? "user" : "system", p.mode);
    return { runId };
  }

  /** One planning run at a time (D13). The check and insert run without an await in between. */
  async startRun(question: string, kind: RunKind, role: "user" | "system" = "user", mode: RunMode = "ai"): Promise<string> {
    if (!this.incident()) throw new Error("not_initialized");
    if (this.activeRunId()) throw new Error("run_active");
    const seq = (this.sql<{ seq: number | null }>`SELECT MAX(seq) AS seq FROM runs`[0]?.seq ?? 0) + 1;
    const runId = `run-${seq}`;
    const now = Date.now();
    this.sql`INSERT INTO runs (id, seq, kind, question, status, mode, started_at) VALUES (${runId}, ${seq}, ${kind}, ${question}, 'running', ${mode}, ${now})`;
    this.addMessage(runId, role, question);
    this.event("run_started", { runId, kind });
    this.touch();
    this.syncState();
    await this.upsertIndex();
    // Test seam: the integration tests drive some runs by hand (vitest.config.ts sets it); unset everywhere else.
    if ((this.env as { DIAGNOSIS_AUTOSTART?: string }).DIAGNOSIS_AUTOSTART !== "off") await this.launchRun(runId);
    return runId;
  }

  /**
   * Starts the run's DiagnosisWorkflow (TDD §8.1) with ID `diag-<incidentId>-<seq>`. Idempotent. If the
   * Workflow can't start, the run ends as `failed` with a note, so the composer isn't left disabled.
   */
  async launchRun(runId: string): Promise<string | null> {
    const run = this.mustRun(runId);
    const existing = this.sql<{ workflow_id: string | null }>`SELECT workflow_id FROM runs WHERE id = ${runId}`[0]?.workflow_id;
    if (existing) return existing;
    if (run.status !== "running") return null;
    const id = `diag-${this.name}-${run.seq}`;
    try {
      await this.runWorkflow("DIAGNOSIS_WORKFLOW", { incidentId: this.name, runId }, { id });
      this.sql`UPDATE runs SET workflow_id = ${id} WHERE id = ${runId}`;
      return id;
    } catch (e) {
      console.error("could not start the diagnosis workflow", (e as Error).message);
      this.addMessage(runId, "system", "The diagnosis couldn't start. Please try again in a moment.");
      await this.finishRun(runId, "failed");
      return null;
    }
  }

  const viewer = role === "viewer";
  const note = runNote(state.timeline);

  const onSend = async (message: string) => {
    setError(null);
    try {
      await send(message);
      setText("");
    } catch (e) {
      setError((e as Error).message.includes("run_active") ? "Still working on the last question. Try again when it finishes." : "Couldn't send that. Try again.");
    }
  };

  const newIncidentFor = async () => {
    const r = await createIncident(text);
    if (!r.ok) return setError(ERROR_TEXT[r.error]);
    setText("");
    notifyIncidentsChanged();
    navigate(`/i/${r.id}`);
  };

  return (
    // shrink-0: as a flex child of the scrolling <main> it would otherwise shrink to one screen, and the
    // sticky header and composer would scroll away with it.
    <div className="flex min-h-full shrink-0 flex-col">

        <div className="mx-auto flex max-w-3xl flex-wrap items-center gap-2">
          <h1 className="min-w-0 truncate font-mono text-base">{state.target}</h1>

import { cloudflareTest, readD1Migrations } from "@cloudflare/vitest-plugin";
import { defineConfig } from "vitest/config";

// Stand-in for the demo Worker behind the main Worker's DEMO binding. Modes live in memory, and every
// setModes is recorded so tests can assert on what the main Worker asked for (calls()). Its default fetch
// serves /w/<ws>/ by mode (500 when "errors" is on, otherwise 200), so the fake internet can route the test
// demo host here and approval tests see the restore take effect. The real demo is tested in "demo".
const demoStub = `
import { WorkerEntrypoint } from "cloudflare:workers";
const modes = new Map();
const calls = [];
export class DemoAdmin extends WorkerEntrypoint {
  ping() { return "pong"; }
  getModes(ws) { return modes.get(ws) ?? ["healthy"]; }
  setModes(ws, m) { calls.push({ ws, modes: m }); modes.set(ws, m); return m; }
  calls(ws) { return calls.filter((c) => c.ws === ws); }
  fetch(request) { return new Response("demo stub " + new URL(request.url).pathname); }
}
export default {
  fetch(request) {
    const [, w, ws] = new URL(request.url).pathname.split("/");
    if (w !== "w" || !ws) return new Response("demo stub");
    const broken = (modes.get(ws) ?? []).includes("errors");
    return new Response(broken ? "Internal Server Error" : "<html><body>demo ok</body></html>", { status: broken ? 500 : 200, headers: { "content-type": "text/html" } });
  },
};
`;

// The integration project's only "internet" (outboundService): DoH answers for *.example.com and the test
// demo host, so Workflow runs are offline and deterministic. nxdomain.example.com doesn't exist,
// broken.example.com returns 500, every other *.example.com host serves a small HTML page, and the test demo
// host is served by the demo stub (through its DEMO binding here).
const DEMO_HOST = "incident-copilot-demo.test.workers.dev";
const fakeInternet = `
const dns = (body) => new Response(JSON.stringify(body), { headers: { "content-type": "application/dns-json" } });
export default {
  async fetch(request, env) {
    const url = new URL(request.url);
    if (url.hostname === "cloudflare-dns.com" || url.hostname === "dns.google") {
      const name = (url.searchParams.get("name") || "").replace(/\\.$/, "");
      const known = name.endsWith("example.com") || name === "${DEMO_HOST}";
      if (!known || name === "nxdomain.example.com") return dns({ Status: 3 });
      const a = url.searchParams.get("type") === "A";
      return dns({ Status: 0, Answer: a ? [{ name, type: 1, TTL: 300, data: "93.184.215.14" }] : [] });
    }
    if (url.hostname === "${DEMO_HOST}") return env.DEMO.fetch(request);
    if (url.hostname === "broken.example.com") return new Response("upstream exploded", { status: 500 });
    if (url.hostname.endsWith("example.com")) return new Response("<html><body>ok</body></html>", { headers: { "content-type": "text/html" } });
    return new Response("fake internet: unknown host " + url.hostname, { status: 502 });
  },
};
`;

export default defineConfig({
  test: {
    projects: [
      {
        test: { name: "unit", include: ["test/unit/**/*.test.ts"], environment: "node" },
      },
      {
        plugins: [
          cloudflareTest(async () => ({
            wrangler: { configPath: "./wrangler.jsonc" },
            // The AI binding is remote by default: tests must not need a login or spend neurons.
            remoteBindings: false,
            miniflare: {
              bindings: {

              outboundService: "fake-internet",
              workers: [

# `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`

The Workers Vitest integration allows you to write Vitest tests that run inside the Workers runtime.
Refer to the [documentation](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/testing/vitest-integration/) and [examples](https://github.com/cloudflare/workers-sdk/tree/main/fixtures/vitest-plugin-examples/) for more information.

- ✅ Supports both **unit tests** and **integration tests**
- 📚 Provides direct access to Workers runtime APIs and bindings
- 📦 Implements isolated per-test storage
- 🔥 Runs tests fully-locally using [Miniflare](https://miniflare.dev/)
- ⚡️ Leverages Vitest's hot-module reloading for near instant reruns
- ↩️ Provides a declarative interface for mocking outbound requests
- 🧩 Supports projects with multiple Workers

### Banner

Full-width message bar with an optional trailing CTA slot.

**Type:** component

**Import:** `import { Banner } from "@cloudflare/kumo";`

**Category:** Feedback

**Props:**

- `className`: string
  Additional CSS classes merged via `cn()`.
- `id`: string
- `lang`: string
- `icon`: ReactNode
  Icon element rendered before the banner content (e.g. from `@phosphor-icons/react`).
- `title`: string
  Primary heading text for the banner. Use for i18n string injection.
- `description`: ReactNode
  Secondary description text displayed below the title. Use for i18n string injection.
- `action`: ReactNode
  Action slot for a CTA button or link. Compact banners render a Kumo `Link` inline with the description; CTAs render at the trailing end. Use `Banner.Action` for accent-aware CTAs that self-style to the banner variant; other nodes are rendered as-is. Multiple actions can be passed in a Fragment. Only used in structured mode (with `title` or `description`).
- `text`: string
- `children`: ReactNode
- `variant`: enum [default: default]
  - `"default"`: Informational banner for general messages
  - `"alert"`: Warning banner for cautionary messages
  - `"error"`: Error banner for critical issues
  - `"secondary"`: Neutral banner for secondary messages
- `size`: enum [default: base]
  - `"base"`: Default banner size
  - `"sm"`: Compact banner for dialogs and tight spaces

**Colors (kumo tokens used):**

`bg-kumo-contrast`, `bg-kumo-danger-tint`, `bg-kumo-info-tint`, `bg-kumo-warning-tint`, `fill-kumo-danger`, `fill-kumo-info`, `fill-kumo-interact`, `fill-kumo-warning`, `text-kumo-danger`, `text-kumo-default`, `text-kumo-info`, `text-kumo-warning`

**Sub-Components:**

This is a compound component. Use these sub-components:

#### Banner.Action

Action sub-component

### Toasty

Toasty — toast notification provider and viewport.

**Type:** component

**Import:** `import { Toasty } from "@cloudflare/kumo";`

**Category:** Feedback

**Props:**

- `variant`: enum [default: default]
  - `"default"`: Default toast style
  - `"success"`: Success toast for confirmations and positive outcomes
  - `"error"`: Error toast for critical issues
  - `"warning"`: Warning toast for cautionary messages
  - `"info"`: Info toast for neutral informational messages
- `children`: React.ReactNode (required)
  Application content. Toasts render via a portal above this.
- `container`: PortalContainer
  Container element for the portal. Use this to render toasts inside a Shadow DOM or custom container. Overrides `KumoPortalProvider` context.
- `toastManager`: ReturnType<typeof createKumoToastManager>
  Optional toast manager created by `createKumoToastManager()`. When provided, allows code outside the React tree (timers, module-load callbacks, query-cache listeners) to dispatch toasts via the same dedupe-aware manager that `useKumoToastManager()` returns inside the tree. Forwarded to the underlying `@base-ui/react/toast` `Toast.Provider` `toastManager` prop — see https://base-ui.com/react/components/toast for the upstream primitive.

**Colors (kumo tokens used):**

`bg-kumo-base`, `bg-kumo-control`, `bg-kumo-danger-tint`, `bg-kumo-info-tint`, `bg-kumo-success-tint`, `bg-kumo-warning-tint`, `border-kumo-fill`, `ring-kumo-danger`, `ring-kumo-hairline`, `ring-kumo-info`, `ring-kumo-line`, `ring-kumo-success`, `ring-kumo-warning`, `text-kumo-danger`, `text-kumo-default`, `text-kumo-info`, `text-kumo-subtle`, `text-kumo-success`, `text-kumo-warning`

     2	import { forwardRef, useEffect } from "react";

    10	/** Kumo links (sidebar items, link buttons) navigate inside the app instead of reloading. */
    11	const AppLink = forwardRef<HTMLAnchorElement, LinkComponentProps>(({ href, to, ...rest }, ref) => <Link ref={ref} href={href ?? to ?? "/"} {...rest} />);
    12	AppLink.displayName = "AppLink";
    13	
    14	export function App() {
    15	  // Creates the workspace cookie up front, so the first WebSocket already carries it.
    16	  useEffect(() => void getSession(), []);
    17	  return (
    18	    <LinkProvider component={AppLink}>

    21	      </AppShell>

If refused, `retryAfter = 60`, because the binding doesn't report when it resets.\n  - Then the hourly check: a D1 fixed window (`usage_counters`, kind `run`, `window_start = floor(now / 1h)`), using `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET count = count + 1 RETURNING count`. If `count > LIMITS.runsPerHour`, then `retryAfter = seconds until the window ends`.\n  - Fails open: a binding or D1 error is logged and the run is allowed. This matches the existing \"accounting never breaks a diagnosis\" rule. The guard and the Workers AI allocation error still protect neurons.\n- A generic `takeWindow(db, ws, kind, limit, windowMs, now)` underneath, so WP-23 (voice) and WP-22 (postmortem) can reuse it.\n- `probeLimiter(env, allowOrigin, demoBaseUrl)` → `(host) => Promise<boolean>`. It returns `true` for the demo host and the dev allow-origin host, and otherwise calls `PROBE_LIMIT.limit({ key: host })`, failing open on error.\n\n### 3. Budget guard: `worker/llm/usage.ts`\n- Add `budgetMode(usage: UsageSink): Promise<RunMode>`, which returns `\"rule_based\"` when `today() >= BUDGET_GUARD_AT`. If the read fails, it returns `\"ai\"`, because the LLM path already falls back to rules when the allocation runs out.\n\n### 4. Wire it in\n- **`worker/api/incidents.ts` POST:** replace the `// WP-17` comment with `takeRun`. A refusal returns 429 `{ error: \"rate_limited\", retryAfter }` plus a `Retry-After` header. This happens after target parsing and before `init` (TDD §7.1).\n- **`worker/agent/IncidentAgent.ts`:**\n  - `sendMessage`: check role → `run_active` (so a refused send isn't charged) → `takeRun(env, incident.workspace_id)` → on refusal, throw `rate_limited:<retryAfter>`.\n  - `startRun`: when `mode === \"ai\"`, apply `budgetMode(d1Usage(env.DB))` **before** the synchronous active-run check and insert, so the \"no await between check and insert\" rule still holds. Update the comment in `getRunContext`.\n- **`worker/workflows/diagnosis.ts:43`:** pass `rateLimit: probeLimiter(...)` in the tool context. `quickCheck` (verify/monitor) stays unlimited: it runs only on the demo after an action, and WP-18's cron has its own caps.\n- **`worker/api/session.ts`:** `budgetMode` is now the real value from `budgetMode(d1Usage(c.env.DB))`.\n\n### 5. UI\n- **`web/lib/api.ts`:**\n  - `createIncident` returns `{ ok: false, error: \"rate_limited\", retryAfter }`, read from the body or the `Retry-After` header.\n  - Add `parseRateLimited(message)` for the agent error.\n  - Add `retryText(seconds)`, giving \"about a minute\" / \"N minutes\".\n- **`web/App.tsx`:**\n  - Wrap the app in Kumo `<Toasty>`.\n  - Keep the session in state and refetch it on `INCIDENTS_CHANGED`, so the banner appears without a reload once the budget trips.\n  - Pass `budgetMode` to `AppShell`.\n- **`web/components/BudgetBanner.tsx` (new):** a Kumo `Banner` (variant `alert`, robot icon, text, not colour only) that says: \"Daily AI budget used up. New diagnoses use built-in rules until 00:00 UTC.\" `AppShell` renders it as a full-width row inside `<header>`, under the title bar, only when the mode is `rule_based`. It has to work at 390 px.\n- **`NewIncident.tsx` and `IncidentView.tsx`:** on `rate_limited`, show a warning toast (\"Run limit reached. Try again in …\") through the Kumo toast manager (`useKumoToastManager`), and keep the inline `role=\"alert\"` text so screen readers hear it. The text the user typed stays in the box.\n\n### 6. Tests\n- **`test/integration/quota.test.ts` (new):**\n  - 4 `POST /api/incidents` in a minute from one workspace → the 4th is 429 with `retryAfter` and `Retry-After`.\n  - The hourly D1 window via `takeWindow` with an injected clock: the 11th call is refused, a new hour is allowed, and `retryAfter` counts down to the end of the window.\n  - Another workspace isn't affected.\n  - `sendMessage` past the quota throws `rate_limited`.\n- **Budget guard (integration):**\n  - Set today's `llm_usage` to 9000.\n  - `/api/session` → `budgetMode: \"rule_based\"`.\n  - A new incident's run row has `mode = \"rule_based\"`.\n  - At 8999 → `ai`. Storage is isolated per test, and the row is deleted afterwards anyway.\n- **Unit:** `probeLimiter` skips the demo host and limits others (fake binding); web `api` handles 429 parsing and `retryText`.\n- **`vitest.config.ts`:** override `PROBE_LIMIT` with a high limit in the integration miniflare options (`ratelimits`). The existing diagnosis tests probe the same `*.example.com` hosts many times a minute, and the real limit would make them flaky.\n- **Check existing tests:** `agent.test.ts` calls `sendMessage` 3 times. Confirm it stays under 3 a minute per workspace.\n\n### 7. Docs and board (per memory rules)\n- **TASKS.md:**\n  - WP-17 🔄 at start, ✅ with the hash at the end; tick the checklist.\n  - Add a decisions-log row covering the demo exemption, fail-open behaviour, `retryAfter = 60` for the burst limit, and `quickCheck` not being limited.\n  - Remove the \"Don't share the URL until WP-17\" restriction note once it's ✅.\n- **TDD:** fold the decisions into §11 (and §7.1 for the 429 body).\n- **README:**\n  - Move WP-17 out of \"Designed, not built\".\n  - Add the live URL. TASKS rule: only once WP-17 is ✅. Confirm with the user before committing the URL, because it's public.\n- **PROMPTS.md:** append this prompt and the answer.\n- Commit on `dev`. Locally with `pnpm dev`:\n   - Send 4 `curl POST /api/incidents` with one cookie → the 4th returns 429 with `retryAfter`.\n   - In the browser, send 4 quick messages → toast with the time until reset.\n3. Run `wrangler d1 execute incident-copilot --local --command \"INSERT … llm_usage (today, 9000)\"` → reload → the header banner shows; a new incident runs `rule_based` (0 LLM turns). Delete the row afterwards.\n4. Take browser screenshots of the banner and toast in light, dark and at 390 px (the playwright-core approach from WP-10).\n5. Live: the bindings deploy through the next `dev` → `main` merge (CI). After the deploy, repeat step 2 against the live URL.\n"

## Approved Plan:
# WP-17: Limits + budget guard

## Context

The deployed `workers.dev` URL has no protection yet (accepted risk D2), so it can't be shared until WP-17 lands. This WP adds:
- the three Rate Limiting bindings,
- the per-workspace run quota (3 a minute, 10 an hour; D14),
- the daily neuron guard (at 9,000 or more, runs switch to rule-based),
- the UI for both: a `BudgetBanner` in the header and a toast saying how long until the limit resets.

Done when (TDD §17): 4 runs in a minute → 429 with the time until reset; a forced neuron total → rule-based banner.

The groundwork is already there:
- D1 `usage_counters` and `llm_usage` exist (`migrations/0001_init.sql`), so no migration is needed.
- `d1Usage().today()` is in `worker/llm/usage.ts`, and `BUDGET_GUARD_AT = 9000` is in `worker/loop/budget.ts`.
- `ToolContext.rateLimit` is already called before every `http_probe` sample and returns `partial: "rate_limited"` (`worker/tools/http.ts:65`).
- `runs.mode` / `RunMode` already carries `rule_based` (it's used by the smoke test).
- `IncidentView` already shows a per-incident "rule-based" banner.

**User decision:** probes to our own demo origin are **not** limited. Every user's demo shares one hostname; the limit protects third-party sites, and the run quota still caps demo use.

## Changes

### 1. Bindings: `wrangler.jsonc`
Add `ratelimits` as in TDD §5.1:
- `PROBE_LIMIT`: 10 per 60 s, namespace `1001`
- `RUN_LIMIT`: 3 per 60 s, namespace `1002`
- `VOICE_LIMIT`: 5 per 60 s, namespace `1003` (bound now; WP-23 uses it)

Then run `pnpm cf-typegen` to update `worker-configuration.d.ts`.

### 2. `worker/safety/quota.ts` (new)
- `takeRun(env, workspaceId, now?)` → `{ ok: true } | { ok: false; retryAfter: number }` (retryAfter is in seconds).
  - First the burst check: `env.RUN_LIMIT.limit({ key: ws })`. If refused, `retryAfter = 60`, because the binding doesn't report when it resets.
  - Then the hourly check: a D1 fixed window (`usage_counters`, kind `run`, `window_start = floor(now / 1h)`), using `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET count = count + 1 RETURNING count`. If `count > LIMITS.runsPerHour`, then `retryAfter = seconds until the window ends`.
  - Fails open: a binding or D1 error is logged and the run is allowed. This matches the existing "accounting never breaks a diagnosis" rule. The guard and the Workers AI allocation error still protect neurons.
- A generic `takeWindow(db, ws, kind, limit, windowMs, now)` underneath, so WP-23 (voice) and WP-22 (postmortem) can reuse it.
- `probeLimiter(env, allowOrigin, demoBaseUrl)` → `(host) => Promise<boolean>`. It returns `true` for the demo host and the dev allow-origin host, and otherwise calls `PROBE_LIMIT.limit({ key: host })`, failing open on error.

### 3. Budget guard: `worker/llm/usage.ts`
- Add `budgetMode(usage: UsageSink): Promise<RunMode>`, which returns `"rule_based"` when `today() >= BUDGET_GUARD_AT`. If the read fails, it returns `"ai"`, because the LLM path already falls back to rules when the allocation runs out.

### 4. Wire it in
- **`worker/api/incidents.ts` POST:** replace the `// WP-17` comment with `takeRun`. A refusal returns 429 `{ error: "rate_limited", retryAfter }` plus a `Retry-After` header. This happens after target parsing and before `init` (TDD §7.1).
- **`worker/agent/IncidentAgent.ts`:**
  - `sendMessage`: check role → `run_active` (so a refused send isn't charged) → `takeRun(env, incident.workspace_id)` → on refusal, throw `rate_limited:<retryAfter>`.
  - `startRun`: when `mode === "ai"`, apply `budgetMode(d1Usage(env.DB))` **before** the synchronous active-run check and insert, so the "no await between check and insert" rule still holds. Update the comment in `getRunContext`.
- **`worker/workflows/diagnosis.ts:43`:** pass `rateLimit: probeLimiter(...)` in the tool context. `quickCheck` (verify/monitor) stays unlimited: it runs only on the demo after an action, and WP-18's cron has its own caps.
- **`worker/api/session.ts`:** `budgetMode` is now the real value from `budgetMode(d1Usage(c.env.DB))`.

### 5. UI
- **`web/lib/api.ts`:**
  - `createIncident` returns `{ ok: false, error: "rate_limited", retryAfter }`, read from the body or the `Retry-After` header.
  - Add `parseRateLimited(message)` for the agent error.
  - Add `retryText(seconds)`, giving "about a minute" / "N minutes".
- **`web/App.tsx`:**
  - Wrap the app in Kumo `<Toasty>`.
  - Keep the session in state and refetch it on `INCIDENTS_CHANGED`, so the banner appears without a reload once the budget trips.
  - Pass `budgetMode` to `AppShell`.
- **`web/components/BudgetBanner.tsx` (new):** a Kumo `Banner` (variant `alert`, robot icon, text, not colour only) that says: "Daily AI budget used up. New diagnoses use built-in rules until 00:00 UTC." `AppShell` renders it as a full-width row inside `<header>`, under the title bar, only when the mode is `rule_based`. It has to work at 390 px.
- **`NewIncident.tsx` and `IncidentView.tsx`:** on `rate_limited`, show a warning toast ("Run limit reached. Try again in …") through the Kumo toast manager (`useKumoToastManager`), and keep the inline `role="alert"` text so screen readers hear it. The text the user typed stays in the box.

### 6. Tests
- **`test/integration/quota.test.ts` (new):**
  - 4 `POST /api/incidents` in a minute from one workspace → the 4th is 429 with `retryAfter` and `Retry-After`.
  - The hourly D1 window via `takeWindow` with an injected clock: the 11th call is refused, a new hour is allowed, and `retryAfter` counts down to the end of the window.
  - Another workspace isn't affected.
  - `sendMessage` past the quota throws `rate_limited`.
- **Budget guard (integration):**
  - Set today's `llm_usage` to 9000.
  - `/api/session` → `budgetMode: "rule_based"`.
  - A new incident's run row has `mode = "rule_based"`.
  - At 8999 → `ai`. Storage is isolated per test, and the row is deleted afterwards anyway.
- **Unit:** `probeLimiter` skips the demo host and limits others (fake binding); web `api` handles 429 parsing and `retryText`.
- **`vitest.config.ts`:** override `PROBE_LIMIT` with a high limit in the integration miniflare options (`ratelimits`). The existing diagnosis tests probe the same `*.example.com` hosts many times a minute, and the real limit would make them flaky.
- **Check existing tests:** `agent.test.ts` calls `sendMessage` 3 times. Confirm it stays under 3 a minute per workspace.

### 7. Docs and board (per memory rules)
- **TASKS.md:**
  - WP-17 🔄 at start, ✅ with the hash at the end; tick the checklist.
  - Add a decisions-log row covering the demo exemption, fail-open behaviour, `retryAfter = 60` for the burst limit, and `quickCheck` not being limited.
  - Remove the "Don't share the URL until WP-17" restriction note once it's ✅.
- **TDD:** fold the decisions into §11 (and §7.1 for the 429 body).
- **README:**
  - Move WP-17 out of "Designed, not built".
  - Add the live URL. TASKS rule: only once WP-17 is ✅. Confirm with the user before committing the URL, because it's public.
- **PROMPTS.md:** append this prompt and the answer.
- Commit on `dev`. Don't push to `main`; the user raises the PR.

## Verification
1. `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test` all pass, 3 runs in a row (the earlier flake history makes this worth it).
2. Locally with `pnpm dev`:
   - Send 4 `curl POST /api/incidents` with one cookie → the 4th returns 429 with `retryAfter`.
   - In the browser, send 4 quick messages → toast with the time until reset.
3. Run `wrangler d1 execute incident-copilot --local --command "INSERT … llm_usage (today, 9000)"` → reload → the header banner shows; a new incident runs `rule_based` (0 LLM turns). Delete the row afterwards.
4. Take browser screenshots of the banner and toast in light, dark and at 390 px (the playwright-core approach from WP-10).
5. Live: the bindings deploy through the next `dev` → `main` merge (CI). After the deploy, repeat step 2 against the live URL.

One atomic upsert per call, so two requests can't\n * both read the old count. Every call counts, refused ones too; the window still ends on time.\n */\nexport async function takeWindow(db: D1Database, workspaceId: string, kind: \"run\" | \"voice\", limit: number, windowMs: number, now: number): Promise<Quota> {\n  const windowStart = Math.floor(now / windowMs) * windowMs;\n  const row = await db\n    .prepare(\n      `INSERT INTO usage_counters (workspace_id, kind, window_start, count) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3, 1)\n       ON CONFLICT (workspace_id, kind, window_start) DO UPDATE SET count = count + 1 RETURNING count`,\n    )\n    .bind(workspaceId, kind, windowStart)\n    .first<{ count: number }>();\n  if ((row?.count ?? 0) <= limit) return { ok: true };\n  return { ok: false, retryAfter: Math.max(1, Math.ceil((windowStart + windowMs - now) / 1000)) };\n}\n\n/**\n * The single \"LLM run\" quota (D14): new incidents and follow-ups, 3 a minute (Rate Limiting binding) and\n * 10 an hour (D1) per workspace. Fails open: a binding or D1 error is logged and the run goes ahead, since the\n * budget guard and the Workers AI allocation still protect neurons.\n */\nexport async function takeRun(env: Pick<Env, \"RUN_LIMIT\" | \"DB\">, workspaceId: string, now: number = Date.now()): Promise<Quota> {\n  try {\n    // The binding doesn't say when its window resets, so the burst answer is the worst case: a minute.\n    if (!(await env.RUN_LIMIT.limit({ key: workspaceId })).success) return { ok: false, retryAfter: MINUTE_MS / 1000 };\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(\"RUN_LIMIT failed; allowing the run\", (e as Error).message);\n  }\n  try {\n    return await takeWindow(env.DB, workspaceId, \"run\", LIMITS.runsPerHour, HOUR_MS, now);\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(\"run quota (D1) failed; allowing the run\", (e as Error).message);\n    return { ok: true };\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * `ToolContext.rateLimit` backed by PROBE_LIMIT, keyed by hostname (TDD §11). Our own demo Worker (and the\n * dev allow-origin) isn't limited: every workspace's demo shares one hostname, and the limit exists to protect\n * other people's sites. Fails open, like the run quota.\n */\nexport function probeLimiter(env: Pick<Env, \"PROBE_LIMIT\" | \"DEMO_BASE_URL\">): (host: string) => Promise<boolean> {\n  const exempt = new Set([env.DEMO_BASE_URL, devAllowOrigin(env)].flatMap((u) => (u && URL.canParse(u) ? [new URL(u).hostname] : [])));\n  return async (host) => {\n    if (exempt.has(host)) return true;\n    try {\n      return (await env.PROBE_LIMIT.limit({ key: host })).success;\n    } catch (e) {\n      console.error(\"PROBE_LIMIT failed; allowing the probe\", (e as Error).message);\n      return true;\n    }\n  };\n}\n"
}

import { env, exports } from "cloudflare:workers";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

const BASE = "https://copilot.example.com";

function wsCookie(res: Response): string | undefined {
  return res.headers.getSetCookie().find((c) => c.startsWith("__Host-ws="));
}

describe("workspace cookie + GET /api/session", () => {
  it("creates a workspace on first visit", async () => {
    const res = await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`);
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);

    const cookie = wsCookie(res);
    expect(cookie).toMatch(/^__Host-ws=[0-9a-f]{32};/);
    expect(cookie).toContain("Max-Age=31536000");
    expect(cookie).toContain("Path=/");
    expect(cookie).toContain("HttpOnly");
    expect(cookie).toContain("Secure");
    expect(cookie).toContain("SameSite=Lax");
    expect(cookie).not.toContain("Domain");

    const id = cookie!.slice("__Host-ws=".length, "__Host-ws=".length + 32);
    const body = await res.json<{ demoUrl: string; budgetMode: string; limits: Record<string, number> }>();
    expect(body.demoUrl).toBe(`${env.DEMO_BASE_URL}/w/${id}/`);
    expect(body.budgetMode).toBe("ai");
    expect(body.limits).toMatchObject({ runsPerMinute: 3, runsPerHour: 10 });

    const row = await env.DB.prepare("SELECT id FROM workspaces WHERE id = ?").bind(id).first();
    expect(row).toEqual({ id });
  });

  it("keeps an existing workspace without re-setting the cookie", async () => {
    const first = await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`);
    const id = wsCookie(first)!.split(";")[0]!.split("=")[1]!;
    const { demoUrl } = await first.json<{ demoUrl: string }>();

    const again = await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`, { headers: { cookie: `__Host-ws=${id}` } });
    expect(wsCookie(again)).toBeUndefined();
    expect((await again.json<{ demoUrl: string }>()).demoUrl).toBe(demoUrl);
  });

  it("replaces a malformed cookie", async () => {
    const res = await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`, { headers: { cookie: "__Host-ws=not-an-id" } });
    expect(wsCookie(res)).toMatch(/^__Host-ws=[0-9a-f]{32};/);
  });

  it("ignores a cookie without the __Host- prefix", async () => {
    const res = await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`, {
      headers: { cookie: "ws=0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef" },
    });
    expect(wsCookie(res)).toBeDefined();
  });
});

async function errorOf(p: PromiseLike<unknown>): Promise<string> {
  try {
    await p;
  } catch (e) {
    return (e as Error).message;
  }
  throw new Error("expected the call to fail");
}

}

import { exports } from "cloudflare:workers";

export type Frame = { type: string; [k: string]: unknown };

/** A raw agent WebSocket that speaks the SDK's frames: `cf_agent_state`, `rpc`, plus our stream frames. */
export async function connect(incidentId: string, workspaceId?: string) {
  const res = await exports.default.fetch(`https://copilot.example.com/agents/incident-agent/${incidentId}`, {
    headers: { Upgrade: "websocket", ...(workspaceId ? { Cookie: `__Host-ws=${workspaceId}` } : {}) },
  });
  if (res.status !== 101 || !res.webSocket) throw new Error(`upgrade failed: ${res.status}`);
  const ws = res.webSocket;
  ws.accept();
  const frames: Frame[] = [];
  const waiters: { match: (f: Frame) => boolean; resolve: (f: Frame) => void }[] = [];
  let closed: { code: number; reason: string } | null = null;
  ws.addEventListener("message", (e) => {
    const frame = JSON.parse(String(e.data)) as Frame;
    frames.push(frame);
    for (const w of [...waiters]) {
      if (w.match(frame)) {
        waiters.splice(waiters.indexOf(w), 1);
        w.resolve(frame);
      }
    }
  });
  ws.addEventListener("close", (e) => {
    closed = { code: e.code, reason: e.reason };
  });

  "content": "import { env, exports } from \"cloudflare:workers\";\nimport { describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\";\nimport { incidentAgent } from \"../../worker/agent/stub\";\nimport { newId } from \"../../worker/lib/ids\";\nimport { takeRun, takeWindow } from \"../../worker/safety/quota\";\n\nconst BASE = \"https://copilot.example.com\";\nconst HOUR = 3_600_000;\n\nasync function workspace(): Promise<string> {\n  const res = await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`);\n  return res.headers.getSetCookie().find((c) => c.startsWith(\"__Host-ws=\"))!.split(\";\")[0]!;\n}\n\nconst create = (cookie: string, message = \"diagnose shop.example.com\") =>\n  exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/incidents`, {\n    method: \"POST\",\n    headers: { Cookie: cookie, \"content-type\": \"application/json\" },\n    body: JSON.stringify({ message }),\n  });\n\nconst allowAll = { limit: async () => ({ success: true }) } as unknown as RateLimit;\nconst broken = { limit: async () => Promise.reject(new Error(\"binding down\")) } as unknown as RateLimit;\n\ndescribe(\"run quota (D14)\", () => {\n  it(\"refuses the 4th new incident in a minute with the time until reset; other workspaces are unaffected\", async () => {\n    const mine = await workspace();\n    for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) expect((await create(mine)).status).toBe(200);\n\n    const res = await create(mine);\n    expect(res.status).toBe(429);\n    expect(res.headers.get(\"Retry-After\")).toBe(\"60\");\n    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ error: \"rate_limited\", retryAfter: 60 });\n\n    expect((await create(await workspace())).status).toBe(200);\n  });\n\n  it(\"doesn't charge a message that can't become an incident\", async () => {\n    const mine = await workspace();\n    for (let i = 0; i < 4; i++) expect((await create(mine, \"everything is broken\")).status).toBe(400);\n    expect((await create(mine)).status).toBe(200);\n  });\n\n  it(\"allows 10 runs an hour, then counts down to the end of the window\", async () => {\n    const ws = newId();\n    const start = Date.UTC(2030, 0, 1, 10, 0, 0);\n    for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) expect(await takeRun({ RUN_LIMIT: allowAll, DB: env.DB }, ws, start + i * 1000)).toEqual({ ok: true });\n    expect(await takeRun({ RUN_LIMIT: allowAll, DB: env.DB }, ws, start + 20 * 60_000)).toEqual({ ok: false, retryAfter: 40 * 60 });\n    expect(await takeRun({ RUN_LIMIT: allowAll, DB: env.DB }, ws, start + HOUR)).toEqual({ ok: true });\n  });\n\n  it(\"keeps windows per workspace and kind\", async () => {\n    const [a, b] = [newId(), newId()];\n    const now = Date.UTC(2030, 0, 2);\n    expect(await takeWindow(env.DB, a, \"voice\", 1, HOUR, now)).toEqual({ ok: true });\n    expect(await takeWindow(env.DB, a, \"voice\", 1, HOUR, now)).toMatchObject({ ok: false });\n    expect(await takeWindow(env.DB, a, \"run\", 1, HOUR, now)).toEqual({ ok: true });\n    expect(await takeWindow(env.DB, b, \"voice\", 1, HOUR, now)).toEqual({ ok: true });\n  });\n\n  it(\"fails open when the binding or D1 errors\", async () => {\n    const db = { prepare: () => { throw new Error(\"D1 down\"); } } as unknown as D1Database;\n    expect(await takeRun({ RUN_LIMIT: broken, DB: db }, newId())).toEqual({ ok: true });\n  });\n\n  it(\"counts follow-ups; a follow-up while a run is active isn't charged\", async () => {\n    const ws = newId();\n    const agent = await incidentAgent(env, newId());\n    const { runId } = await agent.init({ workspaceId: ws, target: \"https://shop.example.com/\", origin: \"chat\", message: \"why?\" });\n\n    await expect(agent.sendMessage(\"and now?\")).rejects.toThrow(\"run_active\");\n    const counted = await env.DB.prepare(\"SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM usage_counters WHERE workspace_id = ?\").bind(ws).first<{ n: number }>();\n    expect(counted?.n).toBe(0);\n\n    await agent.finishRun(runId!, \"done\");\n    for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) expect((await takeRun(env, ws)).ok).toBe(true);\n    await expect(agent.sendMessage(\"and now?\")).rejects.toThrow(\"rate_limited:60\");\n  });\n});\n"
}

  "content": "import { env, exports } from \"cloudflare:workers\";\nimport { afterEach, describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\";\nimport { incidentAgent } from \"../../worker/agent/stub\";\nimport { newId } from \"../../worker/lib/ids\";\nimport { BUDGET_GUARD_AT } from \"../../worker/loop/budget\";\n\n// D1 is per test file, so the forced total here can't reach other files' runs; it's cleared after each test.\nconst BASE = \"https://copilot.example.com\";\nconst today = () => new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10);\nconst setToday = (neurons: number) =>\n  env.DB.prepare(\"INSERT INTO llm_usage (day, neurons_est) VALUES (?1, ?2) ON CONFLICT(day) DO UPDATE SET neurons_est = excluded.neurons_est\")\n    .bind(today(), neurons)\n    .run();\n\nasync function budgetMode(): Promise<string> {\n  return (await (await exports.default.fetch(`${BASE}/api/session`)).json<{ budgetMode: string }>()).budgetMode;\n}\n\nasync function firstRunMode(mode?: \"rule_based\"): Promise<string> {\n  const agent = await incidentAgent(env, newId());\n  const { runId } = await agent.init({ workspaceId: newId(), target: \"https://shop.example.com/\", origin: \"chat\", message: \"why?\", ...(mode ? { mode } : {}) });\n  return (await agent.getRunContext(runId!)).mode;\n}\n\ndescribe(\"budget guard (TDD §11)\", () => {\n  afterEach(async () => {\n    await env.DB.prepare(\"DELETE FROM llm_usage WHERE day = ?\").bind(today()).run();\n  });\n\n  it(\"stays on AI below 9,000 neurons today\", async () => {\n    await setToday(BUDGET_GUARD_AT - 1);\n    expect(await budgetMode()).toBe(\"ai\");\n    expect(await firstRunMode()).toBe(\"ai\");\n  });\n\n  it(\"switches new runs and the session to rule-based at 9,000\", async () => {\n    await setToday(BUDGET_GUARD_AT);\n    expect(await budgetMode()).toBe(\"rule_based\");\n    expect(await firstRunMode()).toBe(\"rule_based\");\n    expect(await firstRunMode(\"rule_based\")).toBe(\"rule_based\");\n  });\n});\n"
}

  "content": "import { describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\";\nimport { probeLimiter } from \"../../worker/safety/quota\";\n\nconst DEMO = \"https://incident-copilot-demo.example.workers.dev\";\n\n/** A PROBE_LIMIT stand-in: `limit` successes per key, recording every key it was asked about. */\nfunction fakeLimit(limit: number) {\n  const counts = new Map<string, number>();\n  const binding = {\n    limit: async ({ key }: { key: string }) => {\n      counts.set(key, (counts.get(key) ?? 0) + 1);\n      return { success: counts.get(key)! <= limit };\n    },\n  } as unknown as RateLimit;\n  return { binding, counts };\n}\n\ndescribe(\"probeLimiter\", () => {\n  it(\"limits per hostname\", async () => {\n    const { binding } = fakeLimit(2);\n    const allow = probeLimiter({ PROBE_LIMIT: binding, DEMO_BASE_URL: DEMO });\n    expect([await allow(\"shop.example.com\"), await allow(\"shop.example.com\"), await allow(\"shop.example.com\")]).toEqual([true, true, false]);\n    expect(await allow(\"other.example.com\")).toBe(true);\n  });\n\n  it(\"never limits our own demo or the dev allow-origin, and doesn't count them\", async () => {\n    const { binding, counts } = fakeLimit(0);\n    const allow = probeLimiter({ PROBE_LIMIT: binding, DEMO_BASE_URL: DEMO, SSRF_DEV_ALLOW_ORIGIN: \"http://localhost:5173\" } as Pick<Env, \"PROBE_LIMIT\" | \"DEMO_BASE_URL\">);\n    expect(await allow(\"incident-copilot-demo.example.workers.dev\")).toBe(true);\n    expect(await allow(\"localhost\")).toBe(true);\n    expect(counts.size).toBe(0);\n    expect(await allow(\"shop.example.com\")).toBe(false);\n  });\n\n  it(\"ignores a placeholder demo URL and fails open when the binding errors\", async () => {\n    const binding = { limit: async () => Promise.reject(new Error(\"down\")) } as unknown as RateLimit;\n    expect(await probeLimiter({ PROBE_LIMIT: binding, DEMO_BASE_URL: \"https://incident-copilot-demo.<sub>.workers.dev\" })(\"shop.example.com\")).toBe(true);\n  });\n});\n"
}

describe("runNote", () => {
  const ev = (id: number, type: string, status?: string): TimelineView => ({ id, ts: id, type, detail: status ? { runId: "run-1", status } : { runId: "run-1" } });
  it("names the limit that ended the latest run", () => {

});

The asset sub-probe and extra hosts in a redirect chain only run if room is left, and samples 2–3 are skipped after a loop, hop cap or refusal. Hitting the cap gives a partial result (`partial: "budget"`), not an error. |
428:1. `POST /api/incidents {message}`. The Worker parses the target (D9), checks the run quota (D14, from WP-18), creates the ID, and calls `agent.init(...)`.
436:ctx = step.do("context", agent.getRunContext)                 // incl. budget mode
438:for turn in 1..MAX_PLAN_TURNS (while deadline/budget/tool-cap allow):
462:Tool steps retry with `{limit: 2}` because each retry spends subrequest budget. LLM steps have the model fallback inside the step. `finishRun` runs before the approval wait, so the input is enabled again while waiting (D13).
464:**Limits keep progress (user requirement, 2026-10-15).** Hitting any limit ends the run with the best diagnosis from what was already found. It never throws work away or fails the run. The limits are turns, tool calls, the 60 s deadline, the subrequest budget, `max_tokens`, the neuron allocation and the LLM being unavailable.
466:- **Turn or tool cap:** the last planning turn offers only `submit_findings` (`llmTools({finishOnly: true})`), so the model sums up what it has. If the tool budget runs out early, that finish turn comes next, as long as the LLM is available and budget is left.
467:- **Deadline, subrequest budget, `LlmUnavailable`, allocation:** go straight to findings. Use `classify` over all evidence gathered so far, merged with any usable part of the model's last `submit_findings` through `salvageFindings` (`loop/findings.ts`). The rule path only runs missing checks if budget is left; otherwise it classifies what exists.
470:- **The run records why it stopped** (`runs.status`: `done` · `limit_turns` · `limit_tools` · `limit_deadline` · `limit_budget` · `llm_unavailable`), plus a timeline event. The UI says which limit hit and how many checks the diagnosis uses.
476:- **Statuses.** A finish-only turn is forced by the turn cap (the last turn), the tool cap, or not enough budget for another tool plus a turn. Its status is `limit_turns` / `limit_tools` / `limit_budget` even when the model then submits good findings. Once the deadline passes there are **no more LLM turns**: rules + salvage, `limit_deadline`. A second invalid turn goes to the rules with status `done`.
477:- **Rule path.** It runs only the baseline checks (`dns_lookup` host, `http_probe` target) that this run hasn't done, if budget and time allow. Then the agent's `ruleFindings(runId, lastFindingsArgs)` = `classify` over all stored checks + `salvageFindings` (raw evidence never leaves the DO).
482:- `PLAN_MAX_TOKENS` = 1500, because gpt's reasoning shares the budget.
491:- **Budget.** `tailReserve` is now 8: rules, findings, narrate and finish, plus propose, outcome, action and verify. Each of these is one agent call. **The verify and monitor quick checks run inside the agent** (`afterAction`, `monitorTick`), so their up to 10 fetches don't use the Workflow's subrequest budget.
557:- checks the 160 KB size cap and the limit (from WP-18), base64-encodes the audio and runs whisper,

- **Projects** (`vitest.config.ts`): `unit` runs in node. `integration` loads `wrangler.jsonc` with `LLM_MODE=mock` and a valid test `DEMO_BASE_URL`, and with `remoteBindings: false`, so the AI binding never goes remote: tests need no login and spend no neurons. `demo` loads `demo-service/wrangler.jsonc`.
- **Fake internet (WP-09):** the integration project's `outboundService` is an inline `fake-internet` Worker. It answers DoH for `*.example.com` (NXDOMAIN for `nxdomain.example.com`), returns 500 for `broken.example.com` and serves HTML for other `*.example.com` hosts, so no integration test touches the real network. `diagnosis.test.ts` runs real local Workflows through `introspectWorkflow`; `plan.test.ts` covers every limit with a fake `io`.
- Unit tests load the recorded fixtures as JSON imports and the `.sse` files with Vite `?raw` imports (`test/unit/raw.d.ts`), because the worker tsconfig has no Node types.
Today, Terraform isn't built (WP-25, below the cut line). D1 was created with `wrangler d1 create`, and the AI Gateway in the dashboard (only a gateway named `default` is created automatically). Whether the Terraform provider can manage AI Gateway is still an open question (PRD R1). CI's Terraform steps switch on by themselves once `infra/*.tf` exists.

## Designed, not built (yet)

The full design for each of these is in the TDD. Day 2 of the build is still in progress, so items leave this list as they ship ([`TASKS.md`](TASKS.md) has live status).

| WP | Feature | Design |
|---|---|---|
| 17 | Rate limits (probes, runs, voice) via Rate Limiting bindings, a D1 hourly quota, and a neuron budget guard that switches to rule-based mode at 9,000/day, with a banner | TDD §11 |
| 18 | Watchlist: a cron every minute checks due watches; on failure, `WatchWorkflow` confirms 2 of 3 and opens an incident on its own | TDD §8.5 |
| 19 | Notifications: unread badges, browser notifications, and Slack/Discord webhooks (AES-GCM encrypted, sent once per state change by `NotifyWorkflow`) | TDD §8.5, §11 |
| 20 | LLM eval suite: break the demo each way and score category, citation and proposed action (target ≥ 4/5) | TDD §16 |
| 21 | `cert_info` (crt.sh) and `provider_status` (status pages) tools | TDD §7.3 |
| 22 | Postmortem: streamed markdown summary of a resolved incident | TDD §8.7 |
| 23 | Push-to-talk voice via Whisper, normalizing "dot" and "slash" | TDD §8.7 |
| 24 | `recheck_burst`: re-check every minute for 15 minutes | TDD §8.4 |
- **Read-only probing:** GET/HEAD only, no user-supplied headers, bodies or cookies, and reads capped at 16 KB.
- **Prompt injection:** fetched content goes to the model inside `<untrusted>` tags and is truncated. The model's tool calls are checked with zod and against the origin pin. Actions come only from a fixed catalog, and every action needs a human's approval.
- **Incident links are view-only** for anyone but the workspace that owns them.
- **The smoke flag:** `POST /api/incidents?smoke=rule` runs a diagnosis without the LLM, for the deploy check. It's public, but it can only make a run cheaper.

**Accepted risks:**
- **D2: no rate limits yet.** Rate limits, quotas and the neuron budget guard are WP-17, so the `workers.dev` URL isn't shared until they ship. If it's ever abused, `LLM_MODE` can be set to `mock` on the deployed Worker.
- **D7: the workspace ID is visible.** The demo lives at `/w/<workspaceId>/`, and that ID is also the session credential. It shows up in a public URL, in prompts and in logs. Someone who learns it can act as that anonymous workspace, which only holds that person's own incidents and demo.
- **One location.** Probes run from whichever Cloudflare location runs the Workflow, so the app doesn't claim anything about regional reachability.
```

(3) A burst refusal says 60 s (the binding doesn't report its reset); the D1 hour gives exact seconds. (4) Guard in `startRun`, before the synchronous check-and-insert. (5) Everything fails open (logged). (6) `quickCheck` isn't probe-limited (demo only, after an approved action). (7) Integration tests raise `PROBE_LIMIT` to 10,000 in the miniflare options. Locally verified: 4th run in a minute → 429 `retryAfter: 60` (5 of 5 repeats); forced 9,000 → banner and a 0-LLM run; screenshots in light, dark and at 390 px. **Folded into TDD §7.1, §8.1, §11, §14.** | Every workspace's demo shares one hostname, so a per-host limit would make users block each other. // src/workers/ratelimit/ratelimit.worker.ts
var RatelimitOptionKeys = ["key", "limit", "period"], RatelimitPeriodValues = [10, 60];
function validate(test, message) {
  if (!test)
    throw new Error(message);
}
var Ratelimit = class {
  fetcher;
  limitVal;
  period;
  constructor(config) {
    this.fetcher = config.fetcher, this.limitVal = config.limit, this.period = config.period;
  }
  // method that counts and checks against the limit, delegating the actual
  // bucket/epoch bookkeeping to the `RateLimiterObject` Durable Object
  async limit(options) {
    validate(
      typeof options == "object" && options !== null,
      "invalid rate limit options"
    );
    let invalidProps = Object.keys(options ?? {}).filter(
      (key2) => !RatelimitOptionKeys.includes(key2)
    );
    validate(
      invalidProps.length == 0,
      `bad rate limit options: [${invalidProps.join(",")}]`
    );
    let {
      key = "",
      limit = this.limitVal,
      period = this.period
    } = options;
    return validate(typeof key == "string", `invalid key: ${key}`), validate(typeof limit == "number", `limit must be a number: ${limit}`), validate(typeof period == "number", `period must be a number: ${period}`), validate(
      RatelimitPeriodValues.includes(period),
      `unsupported period: ${period}`
    ), await (await this.fetcher.fetch("http://ratelimit/limit", {
      method: "POST",
      body: JSON.stringify({ key, limit, period })
    })).json();
  }
};
function ratelimit_worker_default(env) {
  return new Ratelimit(env);
}
export {
  ratelimit_worker_default as default
};

### 🤖 Claude · 22:43:05

WP-17 is built, committed on `dev` (`f636c10`, plus the board update `253bb39`) and pushed. It isn't live yet: the limits reach the deployed site when you merge `dev` into `main`.

**What it does now**
- **Run limit:** each workspace can start 3 diagnoses a minute and 10 an hour. New incidents and follow-ups both count. The next one gets a "Run limit reached, try again in about a minute" pop-up, and the same text shows above the message box.
- **Daily AI budget:** once today's AI use reaches 9,000 of the 10,000 free neurons, new diagnoses use the built-in rules instead of AI. A banner in the header says so.
- **Probe limit:** a site can be probed 10 times a minute at most. As you chose, our own demo service is exempt.
- **If a limit check itself fails** (for example the database errors), the run goes ahead rather than blocking users. The daily AI budget still protects cost.

**How I checked it**
- 536 tests pass, 3 runs in a row; that includes new tests for both "done when" checks.
- In the real app locally:
  - The 4th run in a minute was refused with "try again in 60 s": 5 of 5 times with curl and 8 of 8 in the browser.
  - With the AI total forced to 9,000, the banner showed and a new diagnosis ran without AI. I put the local total back to 2,855 afterwards.
- I looked at screenshots in light mode, dark mode and phone width.

**Two older problems I found (not fixed)**
1. **First visit creates several workspaces at once.** The page makes 3 server calls together before it has a cookie, so each call creates its own workspace and the last cookie to arrive wins. My first browser checks missed the 429 because of this, not because of the limit. The visible effect for users is that the demo panel can show a demo link for the wrong workspace.
2. **The run limit can be skipped.** Someone who drops the cookie gets a new workspace on every request. The daily AI budget is the real overall cap on cost. Adding a per-IP limit next to the per-workspace one would close this.

Both are written up on the board, and I can fix either.

Your next steps:
- Open the `dev` → `main` PR. After the deploy, the README can carry the live URL. I left it out because putting a public URL in the repo is your call.
- Tell me whether to fix either of the two problems above.

Lets fix the older probelms as well.

### 🤖 Claude · 22:45:58

I'll fix both. My approach:

1. **Several workspaces on first visit:** the app makes one session call first and waits for it, so only one workspace is ever created. Everything else (sidebar, demo panel, chat) loads after the cookie exists.
2. **Skipping the limit by dropping the cookie:** I'll add a second limit per visitor IP address, next to the per-workspace one. It's a bit looser so two people on the same office network don't block each other: 6 a minute and 20 an hour per IP. The IP is stored only as a hash.

  // ---- roles (D8) ----

  private roleFor(ctx: ConnectionContext): ConnectionRole {
    const ws = workspaceFromCookieHeader(ctx.request.headers.get("cookie"));
    const incident = this.incident();
    return incident && ws === incident.workspace_id ? "owner" : "viewer";
  }

  override shouldConnectionBeReadonly(_connection: Connection, ctx: ConnectionContext): boolean {
    return this.roleFor(ctx) !== "owner";
  }

  override async onConnect(connection: Connection, ctx: ConnectionContext) {
    if (!this.incident()) {
      connection.close(4404, "Unknown incident");
      return;
    }
    connection.setState({ role: this.roleFor(ctx) } satisfies ConnectionInfo);
  }

    await this.upsertIndex();

/** User-facing limits returned by /api/session; enforcement lands in WP-17 (runs, voice) and WP-18 (watches). */
export const LIMITS = {
  runsPerMinute: 3,
  runsPerHour: 10,
  voicePerHour: 20,
  watchesPerWorkspace: 3,
} as const;
}

/**
 * The single "LLM run" quota (D14): new incidents and follow-ups, 3 a minute (Rate Limiting binding) and
 * 10 an hour (D1) per workspace. Fails open: a binding or D1 error is logged and the run goes ahead, since the
 * budget guard and the Workers AI allocation still protect neurons.
 */
export async function takeRun(env: Pick<Env, "RUN_LIMIT" | "DB">, workspaceId: string, now: number = Date.now()): Promise<Quota> {
  try {
    // The binding doesn't say when its window resets, so the burst answer is the worst case: a minute.
    if (!(await env.RUN_LIMIT.limit({ key: workspaceId })).success) return { ok: false, retryAfter: MINUTE_MS / 1000 };
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("RUN_LIMIT failed; allowing the run", (e as Error).message);
  }
  try {
    return await takeWindow(env.DB, workspaceId, "run", LIMITS.runsPerHour, HOUR_MS, now);
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("run quota (D1) failed; allowing the run", (e as Error).message);
    return { ok: true };
  }
}

/**
 * `ToolContext.rateLimit` backed by PROBE_LIMIT, keyed by hostname (TDD §11). Our own demo Worker (and the
 * dev allow-origin) isn't limited: every workspace's demo shares one hostname, and the limit exists to protect
 * other people's sites. Fails open, like the run quota.
```

</details>

Looser, for shared networks. */\n  runsPerMinutePerIp: 6,\n  runsPerHourPerIp: 20,\n''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\n\np='worker/safety/quota.ts'; s=open(p).read()\nstart=s.index('/**\\n * The single \"LLM run\" quota')\nend=s.index('/**\\n * `ToolContext.rateLimit`')\nnew='''/** Who is asking: the workspace, and the visitor's IP as an `ipKey` (null when the request carries none). */\nexport type Caller = { workspaceId: string; ip: string | null };\n\n/**\n * The D1 and rate-limit key for a visitor IP: `ip:` plus a SHA-256 prefix, so raw addresses aren't stored.\n * Null without an address (local tools and tests that don't send `CF-Connecting-IP`; Cloudflare always sets it).\n */\nexport async function ipKey(ip: string | null | undefined): Promise<string | null> {\n  if (!ip) return null;\n  const digest = new Uint8Array(await crypto.subtle.digest(\"SHA-256\", new TextEncoder().encode(ip)));\n  return `ip:${[...digest.slice(0, 16)].map((b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, \"0\")).join(\"\")}`;\n}\n\n/** One Rate Limiting binding call; an error is logged and allows the run (fail open). */\nasync function burst(binding: RateLimit, key: string, name: string): Promise<boolean> {\n  try {\n    return (await binding.limit({ key })).success;\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(`${name} failed; allowing the run`, (e as Error).message);\n    return true;\n  }\n}\n\n/** One D1 window; an error is logged and allows the run (fail open). */\nasync function hourly(db: D1Database, key: string, limit: number, now: number): Promise<Quota> {\n  try {\n    return await takeWindow(db, key, \"run\", limit, HOUR_MS, now);\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(\"run quota (D1) failed; allowing the run\", (e as Error).message);\n    return { ok: true };\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * The single \"LLM run\" quota (D14): new incidents and follow-ups. Per workspace, 3 a minute (RUN_LIMIT) and\n * 10 an hour (D1). Per visitor IP as well, 6 a minute (RUN_IP_LIMIT) and 20 an hour (D1, keyed by `ipKey`),\n * because a client that drops the cookie gets a new workspace each time. Fails open: a binding or D1 error\n * is logged and the run goes ahead, since the budget guard and the Workers AI allocation still protect neurons.\n */\nexport async function takeRun(env: Pick<Env, \"RUN_LIMIT\" | \"RUN_IP_LIMIT\" | \"DB\">, caller: Caller, now: number = Date.now()): Promise<Quota> {\n  // The binding doesn't say when its window resets, so the burst answer is the worst case: a minute.\n  const minute: Quota = { ok: false, retryAfter: MINUTE_MS / 1000 };\n  if (!(await burst(env.RUN_LIMIT, caller.workspaceId, \"RUN_LIMIT\"))) return minute;\n  if (caller.ip && !(await burst(env.RUN_IP_LIMIT, caller.ip, \"RUN_IP_LIMIT\"))) return minute;\n  const ws = await hourly(env.DB, caller.workspaceId, LIMITS.runsPerHour, now);\n  if (!ws.ok) return ws;\n  return caller.ip ? hourly(env.DB, caller.ip, LIMITS.runsPerHourPerIp, now) : ws;\n}\n\n'''\ns=s[:start]+new+s[end:]\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\n\np='wrangler.jsonc'; s=open(p).read()\nold='''    { \"name\": \"VOICE_LIMIT\", \"namespace_id\": \"1003\", \"simple\": { \"limit\": 5, \"period\": 60 } }'''\nnew='''    { \"name\": \"VOICE_LIMIT\", \"namespace_id\": \"1003\", \"simple\": { \"limit\": 5, \"period\": 60 } },\n    { \"name\": \"RUN_IP_LIMIT\", \"namespace_id\": \"1004\", \"simple\": { \"limit\": 6, \"period\": 60 } }'''\nassert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new)\ns=s.replace(\"// TDD §11: probes per hostname, runs per workspace (bursts; the hourly quota is in D1), voice per workspace.\",\"// TDD §11: probes per hostname, runs per workspace and per visitor IP (bursts; the hourly quotas are in D1), voice.\")\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\n\np='vitest.config.ts'; s=open(p).read()\nold='''                VOICE_LIMIT: { namespace_id: \"1003\", simple: { limit: 5, period: 60 } },'''\nnew=old+'''\n                RUN_IP_LIMIT: { namespace_id: \"1004\", simple: { limit: 6, period: 60 } },'''\nassert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new); open(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\npnpm cf-typegen >/dev/null 2>&1; grep -n \"RUN_IP_LIMIT\" worker-configuration.d.ts",

  });

  it("keeps windows per workspace and kind", async () => {
    const [a, b] = [newId(), newId()];
    const now = Date.UTC(2030, 0, 2);
    expect(await takeWindow(env.DB, a, "voice", 1, HOUR, now)).toEqual({ ok: true });
    expect(await takeWindow(env.DB, a, "voice", 1, HOUR, now)).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
    expect(await takeWindow(env.DB, a, "run", 1, HOUR, now)).toEqual({ ok: true });
    expect(await takeWindow(env.DB, b, "voice", 1, HOUR, now)).toEqual({ ok: true });
  });

  it("fails open when the binding or D1 errors", async () => {
    const db = { prepare: () => { throw new Error("D1 down"); } } as unknown as D1Database;
    expect(await takeRun({ RUN_LIMIT: broken, DB: db }, newId())).toEqual({ ok: true });
  });

  it("counts follow-ups; a follow-up while a run is active isn't charged", async () => {
    const ws = newId();
    const agent = await incidentAgent(env, newId());
    const { runId } = await agent.init({ workspaceId: ws, target: "https://shop.example.com/", origin: "chat", message: "why?" });

    expect(await errorOf(agent.sendMessage("and now?"))).toBe("run_active");
    const counted = await env.DB.prepare("SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM usage_counters WHERE workspace_id = ?").bind(ws).first<{ n: number }>();
    expect(counted?.n).toBe(0);

    await agent.finishRun(runId!, "done");
    for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) expect((await takeRun(env, ws)).ok).toBe(true);
    expect(await errorOf(agent.sendMessage("and now?"))).toBe("rate_limited:60");
  });
});

### 🤖 Claude · 22:50:10

I fixed both problems, committed them on `dev` (`feb4707`) and pushed. All 540 tests pass, and I checked both fixes in the browser.

**1. A first visit now creates one workspace.** The app used to make 3 server calls at once before it had a cookie, so each call created its own workspace. Now it makes one session call first and loads the sidebar, demo panel and chat only after it returns.
- In the browser a first visit now sets 1 cookie instead of 6.
- The demo link in the panel now matches your workspace.
- The browser test that missed the 429 earlier now gets it every time.

**2. Dropping the cookie no longer skips the run limit.** Runs are now also limited per visitor IP address, next to the per-workspace limit:
- **The numbers:** 6 a minute and 20 an hour per IP. I set them looser than the per-workspace limit (3 and 10) so people sharing one network, like an office, don't block each other.
- **Follow-ups count too.** They come in over the live connection, which records the IP when it opens.
- **Only a scrambled version (hash) of the IP is stored, never the address itself.** Someone with database access could still work out an address from the hash. It stays in our own database and isn't shown anywhere.
- **In the browser:** a third browser from the same IP was refused on its first run, even though it had a fresh workspace.

The "Run limit reached" message no longer quotes the numbers, because either limit can be the one that's hit.

This adds one more rate-limit setting to the Cloudflare config, and it goes live with the rest of WP-17 when you merge `dev` into `main`. The README's security section and the board are updated.
