When an incident resolves, the watch's `open_incident_id` is cleared.\n\n*As built (WP-18):*\n- **Files:** `watch/store.ts` (D1), `watch/cron.ts` (`runWatchTick`), `workflows/watch.ts`, `api/watches.ts`, `WatchlistPanel`.\n- **Cron:** one 45-subrequest budget per tick; a watch is checked only if 12 are left (a worst-case quick check plus the RPC or create), and the rest wait for the next tick. Each watch has its own try/catch. A duplicate Workflow ID (`instance.already_exists`) is ignored.\n- **WatchWorkflow confirms early:** the first failed re-check confirms (cron's failure + 1 = 2 of 3), saving 30 s. Two passing re-checks end it.\n- **One incident per failure:** the incident ID is its own step, and `open` claims the watch (`UPDATE … WHERE open_incident_id IS NULL`) **before** `init`. A second Workflow from an overlapping failure loses the claim; a retry of our own step recognises its ID.\n- **Watch incidents start with the system message** \"Scheduled check failed for <url>\". They aren't charged to the run quota, but the budget guard still applies. <details><summary>Tool result</summary>

````
| **Workflow / coordination** | **Workflows**: `DiagnosisWorkflow` (extends the Agents SDK's `AgentWorkflow`) runs each LLM turn and each check as its own durable, retryable step, then waits for human approval with `waitForApproval`. |
| **User input** | A React chat UI (Cloudflare's Kumo design system) served by **Workers Static Assets**, with live updates over a WebSocket straight to the incident's Durable Object. |
| **Memory / state** | **Durable Objects** via the Agents SDK: one `IncidentAgent` per incident with its own SQLite (checks, evidence, messages, approvals, timeline). **D1** holds the workspaces, the incident index per workspace (for the sidebar) and the daily neuron count. |

Also used: Service Bindings with RPC (main Worker → demo Worker), Workers Logs, and the `global_fetch_strictly_public` flag.

## Architecture

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  B["Browser<br/>React + Kumo"] -- "HTTPS /api/*" --> W["Main Worker<br/>Hono router"]
  B -- "WebSocket /agents/incident-agent/:id" --> A["IncidentAgent<br/>Durable Object + SQLite"]
  W -- "init / snapshot" --> A
  W --> D1[("D1<br/>incident index")]
  A -- "runWorkflow · approve / reject" --> F["DiagnosisWorkflow<br/>AgentWorkflow"]
````

### 🤖 Claude · 23:13:37

WP-18 is built, committed on `dev` (`ca576d7`) and pushed. All 565 tests pass, 3 runs in a row.

**What it does**
- **Watchlist in the sidebar:** add a URL or click "Watch my demo". Each row shows the last result in words: Passing, Last check failed, or an "Incident open" link. Limits are 3 watches per workspace and 30 across the app, and each watch lasts 24 hours.
- **Check every minute:** the cron checks the watches that are due, without AI. Real sites are due every 5 minutes; your own demo every minute, as you chose.
- **Incident opens by itself:** when a check fails, a Workflow checks again 30 s later. If that fails too, it opens an incident and starts the diagnosis. Later passing checks move it to mitigated, then resolve it after 3 in a row.
- **"Add to watchlist" card:** a broken real site now gets this card, as you chose. Approving it links the watch to that incident, so it resolves itself once the site recovers. A broken demo still gets "Restore demo" first.

**How I checked it**
I ran the app locally against a real site that always returns 500:
- The incident opened by itself 34 s after the cron check.
- When I pointed the watch at a healthy site, the incident became mitigated after 1 passing check and resolved after 3.
- In the browser, the incident showed up in the sidebar tagged "Watch" within 45 s, with no reload.

The panel looks right in light mode, dark mode and at phone width. One bug turned up and is fixed: long error messages were cut off at the sidebar's edge.

**Not checked yet: the exact demo walkthrough.** A dev server on port 5173 is broken: every request returns "fetch failed". It was started before this session, so I didn't stop it, and the local demo only works through that port. The tests cover the demo rules (every minute, and "Restore demo" winning), but the full demo walkthrough still needs a run on the live site after the next deploy, or locally if you stop or restart that server.

**Also changed:** a broken real site now gets an approval card, so the Workflow waits on it. I updated the existing diagnosis tests so those cards expire immediately.

The next `dev` → `main` merge deploys WP-17 and WP-18 together, including the every-minute cron.

Delete expired watches.\n2. Select the due watches.\n3. Keep one 45-subrequest counter for the whole tick (D12). For each due watch:\n   - **Budget:** stop if fewer than 12 subrequests are left; the rest wait for the next tick.\n   - **Check:** `quickCheck(url, {cap: min(10, left)})`, then charge `subrequests + 1` (the RPC or Workflow create).\n   - **Record:** call `recordCheck`.\n   - **Incident open:** call `incidentAgent(env, open_incident_id).reportWatchCheck(pass, consecutivePasses, summary)`.\n   - **Failure, no incident open:** call `WATCH_WORKFLOW.create({ id: \"watch-<id>-<epochMinute>\", params: { watchId } })`. ## Approved Plan:
# WP-19: Notifications (polling, unread, browser notifications, webhooks, NotifyWorkflow)

## Context

WP-18 made watches open and resolve incidents on their own, but nothing tells the user. WP-19 (PRD §5.6 FR-50–54, TDD D16, D18, §11) adds:
- **in-app:** the sidebar polls every 10 s, unread dots, `markRead`
- **browser notifications**
- **Slack/Discord webhooks**, encrypted at rest and sent by a `NotifyWorkflow`, whose instance ID makes each send at most once per state change

**Done when (TDD §17):** a test Discord webhook fires exactly once per state change.

**User decisions:**
1. **The "opened" webhook waits for the first diagnosis** (at most about 90 s), so it carries the likely cause.
2. **Any incident with a watch attached** sends mitigated/resolved webhooks. That covers watch-opened incidents and chat incidents with an approved "Add to watchlist"; it extends D18.
3. **The user sets the production `WEBHOOK_KEY`** with a one-line command I provide. The key never appears in the chat or the repo.

**Already in place:**
- D1 `webhooks` table (`workspace_id` PK, `kind`, `url_ciphertext` BLOB, `iv` BLOB, `url_hint`, `created_at`).
- `incidents_index.unread`; `narrate` sets `incident.unread = 1`.
- Agent `markRead()` callable (owner only) and `POST /api/incidents/:id/read`. The UI doesn't call either yet.
- `GET /api/incidents` returns `unread`.
- WatchWorkflow has a `// WP-19` marker after "open"; `IncidentAgent.transition()` has a `// WP-19` marker.
- `takeWindow` (`worker/safety/quota.ts`) for the test-send limit.
- The fake-internet Worker in `vitest.config.ts`.
- The `WatchlistPanel` refresh, which stands in for polling until now.

## Changes

### Server
- **`migrations/0002_webhook_origin.sql`:** `ALTER TABLE webhooks ADD COLUMN app_origin TEXT`.
  - The app's origin is recorded when the webhook is saved, so messages can link to the incident without committing the URL (D2).
  - `deploy.yml` already applies migrations.
- **`worker/notify/crypto.ts`:**
  - `webhookKey(env)`: reads the `WEBHOOK_KEY` secret (base64, 32 bytes), or returns null. Like the `devAllowOrigin` pattern, it isn't declared in wrangler, so a deploy without it doesn't fail.
  - `encryptUrl(key, url, workspaceId)` → `{ciphertext, iv}` (AES-GCM-256, 12-byte random IV, AAD = workspaceId).
  - `decryptUrl(...)`.
- **`worker/notify/webhook.ts`:**
  - `parseWebhookUrl(url)` → `{kind: "slack" | "discord"}` or an error. It accepts https only, with:
    - host `hooks.slack.com`, or
    - host `discord.com` / `discordapp.com` with a path starting `/api/webhooks/`;
    - no credentials or port.
  - `maskUrl(url)` → `hooks.slack.com/…/••••abcd`.
  - `formatMessage(kind, {event, target, category, cause, link})` → Slack `{text}` / Discord `{content}`. One short message: event (opened / recovering / resolved), target, likely cause for "opened", and the link.
  - `sendWebhook(url, body)`: POST with `redirect: "manual"` and a 10 s abort.
    - 2xx: sent.
    - 4xx other than 429: give up (no retry; logged).
    - 429, 5xx or a network error: throw, so the Workflow step retries.
  - The URL is never logged and never sent to the LLM.
  - Store helpers: `getWebhook`, `putWebhook`, `deleteWebhook`.
- **`worker/api/webhook.ts` (route `/api/webhook`):**
  - `GET` → `{kind, hint}` or `null`.
  - `PUT {url}` → 200 `{kind, hint}`. Errors: 400 `invalid_webhook` (with a message), 503 `webhooks_unavailable` (no key).
  - `DELETE` → 204.
  - `POST /test` → sends one test message now, not through a Workflow, so the user can check the URL. Limited to 5 an hour through `takeWindow` (kind `"webhook_test"`, extending the union). Returns 200 `{sent: true}`, 502 `{error: "send_failed"}` or 429.
- **`worker/workflows/notify.ts`: `NotifyWorkflow`, a plain `WorkflowEntrypoint`, params `{incidentId, event: "opened" | "mitigated" | "resolved"}`:**
  1. `step.do("load")`: incident snapshot + webhook row. End if there's no webhook.
  2. For `opened`: wait for the first diagnosis. Up to 9 rounds of `step.sleep("10 seconds")` + `step.do("check-n")`, until `activeRunId` is null; after 90 s it sends anyway.
  3. `step.do("send")`: decrypt, format, send; retries 3 with exponential backoff.
- **Triggers** (instance ID `notify-<incidentId>-<event>`; a duplicate ID is ignored, so it's at most once per state):
  - **WatchWorkflow:** a `step.do("notify")` after "open" creates `notify-<id>-opened`.
  - **`IncidentAgent.transition()`:** when `to` is `mitigated` or `resolved` and the incident has a `watch_id`, it creates `notify-<id>-<to>`. Logged, never thrown.
- **Unread on automatic changes (FR-50):** `monitorTick` and `reportWatchCheck` transitions set `incident.unread = 1`. Narration already does; status changes the user made don't.
- **Config:**
  - `wrangler.jsonc`: add workflow `{ name: "notify", binding: "NOTIFY_WORKFLOW", class_name: "NotifyWorkflow" }`, export it from `worker/index.ts`, and run `pnpm cf-typegen`.
  - Local `.dev.vars`: add a random `WEBHOOK_KEY` (local only, gitignored).
  - `vitest.config.ts`: a test `WEBHOOK_KEY` binding.

### Web
- **`web/hooks/useIncidents.ts`:**
  - Polls `listIncidents()` every 10 s while the tab is visible, and right away on `INCIDENTS_CHANGED` and when the tab becomes visible again.
  - Returns the rows and tracks which IDs just turned unread (not on the first load).
  - `IncidentSidebar` uses it instead of its own effect. The `WatchlistPanel` refresh can stay, since it's cheap.
- **`IncidentSidebar`:** an unread row shows a dot **and** the word "New" (not colour alone), with bold text. The group label shows an unread count badge.
- **`IncidentView`:**
  - As the owner, calls the `markRead` callable when it opens, and whenever a new assistant message or status arrives while the tab is visible.
  - Then fires `INCIDENTS_CHANGED`, so the dot clears right away.
- **`web/hooks/useNotifications.ts`:**
  - Permission is asked only from a button click.
  - `notify(row)` → `new Notification("Incident Copilot", {body: "<host> · <status>", tag: id})`. Clicking it focuses the tab and navigates to the incident.
  - It fires for newly unread rows, except the incident open in a visible tab.
- **`web/components/NotificationSettings.tsx` (sidebar, under the Watchlist; the TDD's `WebhookSettings`):**
  - **Browser notifications:** a "Turn on" button, or the text On, or "Blocked in your browser settings".
  - **Webhook:**
    - none saved: a Kumo `SensitiveInput` + Save (errors inline, `role="alert"`)
    - saved: the kind + masked hint, "Send test", "Remove"
    - "webhooks unavailable": an explanatory line
- **`web/lib/api.ts`:** `getWebhook`, `saveWebhook`, `deleteWebhook`, `testWebhook`, plus error text.

### Tests
- **`test/unit/webhook.test.ts`:**
  - allowlist accept/reject: http, other hosts, discord without `/api/webhooks/`, credentials, ports
  - AES-GCM round trip; the wrong AAD (another workspace) fails
  - masking
  - `formatMessage` for Slack and Discord
  - `sendWebhook`'s retry decisions, using a fake fetch
- **Integration, `test/integration/notify.test.ts`:**
  - **Fake internet:** `discord.com` / `hooks.slack.com` record POSTs, readable at `https://discord.com/__calls`.
  - **Webhook API:** PUT/GET/DELETE; the stored row is encrypted (the URL doesn't appear in the ciphertext; the hint is masked); 400 for a bad host; `/test` sends once and is rate-limited.
  - **NotifyWorkflow** (introspect, sleeps off): sends one message per event; the same ID twice → one call; no webhook → no call; 4xx → no retry.
  - **Triggers:** a watched incident's `reportWatchCheck` → mitigated → resolved creates `notify-<id>-mitigated` and `-resolved`; a chat incident without a watch creates none; a WatchWorkflow confirm creates `-opened`.
  - **Unread:** automatic transitions set it; `markRead` clears it.
- **Unit (web):** the `useIncidents` newly-unread diff logic (a pure helper), and the web API client's webhook calls.

### Docs and board
- **TASKS.md:** WP-19 🔄 → ✅ with the hash, checklist ticked, decisions-log row covering:
  - the opened message waiting for the diagnosis
  - any watched incident sends
  - `app_origin` + migration 0002
  - the test-send endpoint
  - the key not declared in wrangler
- **TDD:** fold into §5.1, §6.1, §7.1, §8.5, §11, §12, §14.
- **README:** remove WP-19 from "Designed, not built", add a notifications line, and mention the `WEBHOOK_KEY` setup.
- **PROMPTS.md:** log the prompt and answers.
- Commit and push to `dev`.
- **Final message:** give the user the one-line production key command:

  ```
  openssl rand -base64 32 | npx wrangler secret put WEBHOOK_KEY
  ```

## Verification
1. `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test`, 3 runs in a row.
2. **Local, on a new port (only if the user's dev server is in the way):**
   - Save a webhook pointing at a local stand-in? No: there's no real Discord URL here. Instead, check the UI flow:
     - save is refused for a non-allowlisted URL;
     - an allowlisted Discord-shaped URL saves and shows masked.
   - Only "Send test" would reach Discord, so I won't press it with a fake URL.
   - Watch a failing site → the unread dot and "New" appear within 10 s without a reload → opening the incident clears them.
   - Browser notification: permission granted through Playwright's `grantPermissions`; check that `Notification` was constructed (spy).
3. Screenshots of the sidebar (unread row + notification settings) in light, dark and at 390 px.
4. **Done-when with a real Discord webhook:** that needs the user's own Discord URL and the deployed key. After the merge and the secret is set, the user saves their Discord webhook, presses "Send test", then watches the demo, breaks it and restores it. That should give exactly 3 messages (opened with the cause, recovering, resolved).

**Tool call: `Bash`**

```json
{
  "command": "cat > migrations/0002_webhook_origin.sql <<'EOF'\n-- WP-19: the app's origin, recorded when a webhook is saved, so messages can link to the incident\n-- without committing the deployed URL (D2).\nALTER TABLE webhooks ADD COLUMN app_origin TEXT;\nEOF\nmkdir -p worker/notify && cat > worker/notify/crypto.ts <<'EOF'\n/**\n * Webhook URLs at rest (FR-53, TDD §11): AES-GCM-256 with the `WEBHOOK_KEY` secret (base64, 32 bytes), a 12-byte\n * random IV and the workspace ID as additional data, so a row copied to another workspace won't decrypt.\n */\nexport type Sealed = { ciphertext: Uint8Array; iv: Uint8Array };\n\n/**\n * The key, or null when the secret isn't set: webhooks are then switched off. The secret isn't declared in\n * wrangler.jsonc, so a deploy without it still works (set with `wrangler secret put WEBHOOK_KEY`).\n */\nexport async function webhookKey(env: object): Promise<CryptoKey | null> {\n  const raw = (env as { WEBHOOK_KEY?: unknown }).WEBHOOK_KEY;\n  if (typeof raw !== \"string\" || !raw) return null;\n  let bytes: Uint8Array;\n  try {\n    bytes = Uint8Array.from(atob(raw.trim()), (c) => c.charCodeAt(0));\n  } catch {\n    console.error(\"WEBHOOK_KEY isn't valid base64; webhooks are off\");\n    return null;\n  }\n  if (bytes.length !== 32) {\n    console.error(\"WEBHOOK_KEY must be 32 bytes; webhooks are off\");\n    return null;\n  }\n  return crypto.subtle.importKey(\"raw\", bytes, \"AES-GCM\", false, [\"encrypt\", \"decrypt\"]);\n}\n\nconst enc = new TextEncoder();\n\nexport async function encryptUrl(key: CryptoKey, url: string, workspaceId: string): Promise<Sealed> {\n  const iv = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(12));\n  const ciphertext = await crypto.subtle.encrypt({ name: \"AES-GCM\", iv, additionalData: enc.encode(workspaceId) }, key, enc.encode(url));\n  return { ciphertext: new Uint8Array(ciphertext), iv };\n}\n\n/** Throws if the key, IV, workspace or ciphertext don't match. */\nexport async function decryptUrl(key: CryptoKey, sealed: Sealed, workspaceId: string): Promise<string> {\n  const plain = await crypto.subtle.decrypt({ name: \"AES-GCM\", iv: sealed.iv, additionalData: enc.encode(workspaceId) }, key, sealed.ciphertext);\n  return new TextDecoder().decode(plain);\n}\nEOF\necho ok",
  "description": "Add migration 0002 and the webhook crypto helper"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/sahil/personal/cloudflare-infra-chatbot/worker/notify/webhook.ts",
  "content": "import type { Category } from \"../diagnosis/categories\";\nimport type { Fetch } from \"../lib/doh\";\nimport { decryptUrl, encryptUrl, type Sealed } from \"./crypto\";\n\n/**\n * Slack/Discord webhooks (FR-52 to FR-54, TDD §11). Only two exact hosts, https only. The URL is a credential:\n * it's encrypted at rest, shown masked, never logged and never sent to the LLM.\n */\nexport type WebhookKind = \"slack\" | \"discord\";\nexport type NotifyEvent = \"opened\" | \"mitigated\" | \"resolved\";\n\nconst DISCORD_HOSTS = new Set([\"discord.com\", \"discordapp.com\"]);\nconst MAX_URL = 512;\nconst SEND_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000;\n\nexport function parseWebhookUrl(raw: string): { ok: true; kind: WebhookKind; url: string } | { ok: false; message: string } {\n  const bad = (message: string) => ({ ok: false as const, message });\n  if (raw.length > MAX_URL) return bad(\"That URL is too long for a webhook.\");\n  let u: URL;\n  try {\n    u = new URL(raw.trim());\n  } catch {\n    return bad(\"That isn't a valid URL.\");\n  }\n  if (u.protocol !== \"https:\") return bad(\"Webhook URLs must start with https://.\");\n  if (u.username || u.password || u.port) return bad(\"Webhook URLs can't carry a login or a port.\");\n  if (u.hostname === \"hooks.slack.com\" && u.pathname.length > 1) return { ok: true, kind: \"slack\", url: u.href };\n  if (DISCORD_HOSTS.has(u.hostname) && u.pathname.startsWith(\"/api/webhooks/\")) return { ok: true, kind: \"discord\", url: u.href };\n  return bad(\"Only Slack (hooks.slack.com) and Discord (discord.com/api/webhooks/…) webhook URLs are allowed.\");\n}\n\n/** `hooks.slack.com/…/••••abcd`: enough to recognise it, not enough to use it. */\nexport function maskUrl(url: string): string {\n  const u = new URL(url);\n  const tail = u.pathname.replace(/\\/+$/, \"\").slice(-4);\n  return `${u.hostname}/…/••••${tail}`;\n}\n\nexport type MessageInput = { event: NotifyEvent | \"test\"; target: string; category?: Category | null; cause?: string | null; link?: string | null };\n\n/** One short message. Evidence refs like [http-1] are dropped: they mean nothing outside the app. */\nexport function formatMessage(kind: WebhookKind, m: MessageInput): { text: string } | { content: string } {\n  const bold = (s: string) => (kind === \"slack\" ? `*${s}*` : `**${s}**`);\n  const host = safeHost(m.target);\n  const lines: string[] = [];\n  switch (m.event) {\n    case \"test\":\n      lines.push(`✅ ${bold(\"Incident Copilot test message\")}: this webhook works.`);\n      break;\n    case \"opened\": {\n      lines.push(`🔴 ${bold(`Watch alert: ${host} is failing`)}. An incident was opened and diagnosed.`);\n      const cause = m.cause?.replace(/\\s*\\[[a-z]+-\\d+\\]/g, \"\").trim();\n      if (m.category && m.category !== \"inconclusive\") lines.push(`Likely cause (${m.category.replace(/_/g, \" \")}): ${cause || \"see the incident\"}`);\n      else lines.push(\"The diagnosis was inconclusive; the evidence is in the incident.\");\n      break;\n    }\n    case \"mitigated\":\n      lines.push(`🟡 ${bold(`${host} is recovering`)}: a check passed. It resolves after 3 passing checks in a row.`);\n      break;\n    case \"resolved\":\n      lines.push(`🟢 ${bold(`${host} is resolved`)}: 3 passing checks in a row.`);\n      break;\n  }\n  if (m.link) lines.push(m.link);\n  const text = lines.join(\"\\n\");\n  return kind === \"slack\" ? { text } : { content: text };\n}\n\nexport type SendResult = { sent: true } | { sent: false; permanent: true; status: number };\n\n/** Thrown for failures worth retrying (429, 5xx, network, timeout), so a Workflow step tries again. */\nexport class RetryableSendError extends Error {\n  override name = \"RetryableSendError\";\n}\n\n/**\n * POSTs the message. A 4xx other than 429 is permanent (a deleted or wrong webhook): no retry. Redirects aren't\n * followed, so the request can't be bounced to another host.\n */\nexport async function sendWebhook(url: string, body: object, f: Fetch = (i, init) => fetch(i, init)): Promise<SendResult> {\n  let res: Response;\n  try {\n    res = await f(url, {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      headers: { \"content-type\": \"application/json\" },\n      body: JSON.stringify(body),\n      redirect: \"manual\",\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(SEND_TIMEOUT_MS),\n    });\n  } catch (e) {\n    throw new RetryableSendError(`webhook request failed: ${(e as Error).name}`);\n  }\n  if (res.status >= 200 && res.status < 300) return { sent: true };\n  if (res.status === 429 || res.status >= 500) throw new RetryableSendError(`webhook answered ${res.status}`);\n  return { sent: false, permanent: true, status: res.status };\n}\n\nfunction safeHost(target: string): string {\n  try {\n    const u = new URL(target);\n    return u.pathname.length > 1 ? `${u.hostname}${u.pathname}` : u.hostname;\n  } catch {\n    return target;\n  }\n}\n\n// ---- D1 (`webhooks`, one per workspace) ----\n\ntype WebhookRow = { workspace_id: string; kind: WebhookKind; url_ciphertext: ArrayBuffer; iv: ArrayBuffer; url_hint: string; app_origin: string | null };\n\nexport type WebhookInfo = { kind: WebhookKind; hint: string };\n\nexport async function getWebhookInfo(db: D1Database, workspaceId: string): Promise<WebhookInfo | null> {\n  const row = await db.prepare(\"SELECT kind, url_hint FROM webhooks WHERE workspace_id = ?1\").bind(workspaceId).first<{ kind: WebhookKind; url_hint: string }>();\n  return row ? { kind: row.kind, hint: row.url_hint } : null;\n}\n\n/** Encrypts and stores the URL (replacing any earlier one) with the app origin for incident links. */\nexport async function putWebhook(db: D1Database, key: CryptoKey, p: { workspaceId: string; kind: WebhookKind; url: string; appOrigin: string }): Promise<WebhookInfo> {\n  const sealed = await encryptUrl(key, p.url, p.workspaceId);\n  const hint = maskUrl(p.url);\n  await db\n    .prepare(\n      `INSERT INTO webhooks (workspace_id, kind, url_ciphertext, iv, url_hint, created_at, app_origin) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3, ?4, ?5, ?6, ?7)\n       ON CONFLICT (workspace_id) DO UPDATE SET kind = excluded.kind, url_ciphertext = excluded.url_ciphertext, iv = excluded.iv,\n         url_hint = excluded.url_hint, created_at = excluded.created_at, app_origin = excluded.app_origin`,\n    )\n    .bind(p.workspaceId, p.kind, sealed.ciphertext, sealed.iv, hint, Date.now(), p.appOrigin)\n    .run();\n  return { kind: p.kind, hint };\n}\n\nexport async function deleteWebhook(db: D1Database, workspaceId: string): Promise<void> {\n  await db.prepare(\"DELETE FROM webhooks WHERE workspace_id = ?1\").bind(workspaceId).run();\n}\n\n/** The decrypted webhook for sending, or null if there's none (or it can't be decrypted, which is logged). */\nexport async function loadWebhook(db: D1Database, key: CryptoKey, workspaceId: string): Promise<{ kind: WebhookKind; url: string; appOrigin: string | null } | null> {\n  const row = await db.prepare(\"SELECT * FROM webhooks WHERE workspace_id = ?1\").bind(workspaceId).first<WebhookRow>();\n  if (!row) return null;\n  try {\n    const sealed: Sealed = { ciphertext: new Uint8Array(row.url_ciphertext), iv: new Uint8Array(row.iv) };\n    return { kind: row.kind, url: await decryptUrl(key, sealed, workspaceId), appOrigin: row.app_origin };\n  } catch {\n    console.error(\"a stored webhook couldn't be decrypted (was WEBHOOK_KEY changed?)\", workspaceId);\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\nexport const incidentLink = (appOrigin: string | null, incidentId: string) => (appOrigin ? `${appOrigin}/i/${incidentId}` : null);\n"
}

  "content": "import { WorkflowEntrypoint, type WorkflowEvent, type WorkflowStep } from \"cloudflare:workers\";\nimport { incidentAgent } from \"../agent/stub\";\nimport type { Category } from \"../diagnosis/categories\";\nimport { webhookKey } from \"../notify/crypto\";\nimport { formatMessage, incidentLink, loadWebhook, sendWebhook, type NotifyEvent } from \"../notify/webhook\";\n\nexport type NotifyParams = { incidentId: string; event: NotifyEvent };\nexport type NotifyOutcome = { sent: boolean; reason?: \"no_webhook\" | \"unknown_incident\" | \"rejected\" };\n\n/** \"opened\" waits for the first diagnosis, so the message carries the likely cause (user decision, WP-19). */\nconst DIAGNOSIS_POLLS = 9;\nconst DIAGNOSIS_POLL_GAP = \"10 seconds\";\n\n/** `notify-<incidentId>-<event>`: a second create for the same state is a duplicate, so it sends at most once (D18, FR-54). */\nexport const notifyWorkflowId = (incidentId: string, event: NotifyEvent) => `notify-${incidentId}-${event}`;\n\n/** Starts a NotifyWorkflow; a duplicate ID is ignored. Logged, never thrown: a notification never breaks the caller. */\nexport async function startNotify(env: Pick<Env, \"NOTIFY_WORKFLOW\">, incidentId: string, event: NotifyEvent): Promise<void> {\n  try {\n    await env.NOTIFY_WORKFLOW.create({ id: notifyWorkflowId(incidentId, event), params: { incidentId, event } satisfies NotifyParams });\n  } catch (e) {\n    if (!/already exists|duplicate/i.test((e as Error).message)) console.error(\"could not start the notify workflow\", incidentId, event, (e as Error).message);\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Sends one Slack/Discord message for a watched incident's state change (TDD §8.5, D18). A plain\n * WorkflowEntrypoint: one retried step does the sending, so a crash or a 5xx doesn't lose or repeat the message.\n */\nexport class NotifyWorkflow extends WorkflowEntrypoint<Env, NotifyParams> {\n  override async run(event: WorkflowEvent<NotifyParams>, step: WorkflowStep): Promise<NotifyOutcome> {\n    const env = this.env;\n    const { incidentId, event: kind } = event.payload;\n\n    const workspaceId = await step.do(\"load\", async () => {\n      const row = await env.DB.prepare(\"SELECT workspace_id FROM incidents_index WHERE id = ?1\").bind(incidentId).first<{ workspace_id: string }>();\n      return row?.workspace_id ?? null;\n    });\n    if (!workspaceId) return { sent: false, reason: \"unknown_incident\" };\n    const hasWebhook = await step.do(\"has-webhook\", async () => !!(await env.DB.prepare(\"SELECT 1 FROM webhooks WHERE workspace_id = ?1\").bind(workspaceId).first()));\n    if (!hasWebhook) return { sent: false, reason: \"no_webhook\" };\n\n    if (kind === \"opened\") {\n      for (let n = 1; n <= DIAGNOSIS_POLLS; n++) {\n        const running = await step.do(`diagnosis-${n}`, async () => (await (await incidentAgent(env, incidentId)).getSnapshot()).activeRunId !== null);\n        if (!running) break;\n        await step.sleep(`wait-${n}`, DIAGNOSIS_POLL_GAP);\n      }\n    }\n\n    const result = await step.do(\"send\", { retries: { limit: 3, delay: \"5 seconds\", backoff: \"exponential\" }, timeout: \"30 seconds\" }, async () => {\n      const key = await webhookKey(env);\n      const hook = key ? await loadWebhook(env.DB, key, workspaceId) : null;\n      if (!hook) return { sent: false, reason: \"no_webhook\" as const };\n      const s = await (await incidentAgent(env, incidentId)).getSnapshot();\n      const body = formatMessage(hook.kind, {\n        event: kind,\n        target: s.target,\n        category: s.category as Category | null,\n        cause: s.findings?.cause ?? null,\n        link: incidentLink(hook.appOrigin, incidentId),\n      });\n      // Retryable failures throw here, so the step retries; a 4xx is final.\n      const r = await sendWebhook(hook.url, body);\n      if (!r.sent) console.warn(\"webhook refused the message\", incidentId, kind, r.status);\n      return r.sent ? { sent: true } : { sent: false, reason: \"rejected\" as const };\n    });\n    return result;\n  }\n}\n"
}

Nothing on the first\n * load, so opening the app doesn't announce every old unread incident.\n */\nexport function newlyUnread(previous: ReadonlyMap<string, boolean> | null, rows: IncidentRow[]): IncidentRow[] {\n  if (!previous) return [];\n  return rows.filter((r) => r.unread && previous.get(r.id) !== true);\n}\n\n/**\n * The workspace's incidents (D16): polled every 10 s while the tab is visible, reloaded at once when it becomes\n * visible again, on INCIDENTS_CHANGED, and when `reloadKey` changes (navigation). `onNewUnread` gets each row\n * that has just turned unread, for browser notifications.\n */\nexport function useIncidents(reloadKey: string, onNewUnread?: (rows: IncidentRow[]) => void): IncidentRow[] | null {\n  const [rows, setRows] = useState<IncidentRow[] | null>(null);\n  const seen = useRef<Map<string, boolean> | null>(null);\n  const callback = useRef(onNewUnread);\n  useEffect(() => {\n    callback.current = onNewUnread;\n  });\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    let live = true;\n    const load = () =>\n      void listIncidents().then((r) => {\n        if (!live) return;\n        const fresh = newlyUnread(seen.current, r);\n        seen.current = new Map(r.map((x) => [x.id, x.unread]));\n        setRows(r);\n        if (fresh.length) callback.current?.(fresh);\n      });\n    const onVisible = () => document.visibilityState === \"visible\" && load();\n    load();\n    const timer = setInterval(onVisible, POLL_MS);\n    document.addEventListener(\"visibilitychange\", onVisible);\n    window.addEventListener(INCIDENTS_CHANGED, load);\n    return () => {\n      live = false;\n      clearInterval(timer);\n      document.removeEventListener(\"visibilitychange\", onVisible);\n      window.removeEventListener(INCIDENTS_CHANGED, load);\n    };\n  }, [reloadKey]);\n\n  return rows;\n}\nEOF\npnpm typecheck 2>&1 | tail -3",

null : JSON.stringify(body), { status })) as unknown as typeof fetch;\n\n  it(\"maps results and errors\", async () => {\n    expect(await getWebhook(fake(200, { available: false, webhook: null }))).toEqual({ ok: true, value: { available: false, webhook: null } });\n    expect(await saveWebhook(\"x\", fake(400, { error: \"invalid_webhook\", message: \"Only Slack…\" }))).toEqual({ ok: false, error: \"invalid_webhook\", message: \"Only Slack…\" });\n    expect(await saveWebhook(\"x\", fake(503, { error: \"webhooks_unavailable\" }))).toEqual({ ok: false, error: \"webhooks_unavailable\" });\n    expect(await testWebhook(fake(429, { error: \"rate_limited\", retryAfter: 60 }))).toEqual({ ok: false, error: \"rate_limited\" });\n    expect(await testWebhook(fake(502, { error: \"send_failed\" }))).toEqual({ ok: false, error: \"send_failed\" });\n    expect(await removeWebhook(fake(204))).toEqual({ ok: true, value: null });\n    expect(await getWebhook(fake(500, { error: \"boom\" }))).toEqual({ ok: false, error: \"server\" });\n  });\n});\nEOF\ngrep -n \"Investigating\\|Resolved\" web/lib/incident.ts | head -3; npx vitest run --project unit test/unit/web/notifications.test.ts 2>&1 | grep -E \"Tests |×|Error\"",

100:- **Rate limits and the budget guard (WP-17):** a workspace can start 3 diagnoses a minute (Rate Limiting binding) and 10 an hour (a D1 counter); new incidents and follow-ups both count, and a refusal is a 429 that says when to try again. Because dropping the cookie gives a new workspace, each visitor IP is also limited, to 6 a minute and 20 an hour (only a hash of the IP is stored). Probes are limited to 10 a minute per hostname, so the app can't be used to hammer a site; the app's own demo service is exempt, since every workspace's demo shares one hostname. Once today's estimated Workers AI use reaches 9,000 neurons (of the 10,000 free), new runs use the rule engine instead of the LLM and the header says so. These checks fail open: if the binding or D1 errors, the run goes ahead, and the LLM path still falls back to rules when Workers AI reports the daily allocation is used up.

### 🤖 Claude · 23:56:21

WP-19 is built, committed on `dev` (`8988fd9`) and pushed. All 585 tests pass, 3 runs in a row.

**What it does**
- **Sidebar updates by itself:** it checks for incident changes every 10 s while the tab is open. New activity shows as a dot, bold text, the word "New" and an "n new" count. Opening the incident clears it.
- **Browser notifications:** a "Turn on notifications" button in a new Notifications section of the sidebar. After that, each newly active incident pops one notification, except the one you're already looking at.
- **Slack/Discord webhook:** each workspace can save one. It's stored encrypted, only ever shown masked (`discord.com/…/••••abcd`), and there's a "Send test" button (5 an hour).
- **What gets posted** for a watched incident, once per event:
  - **opened:** sent after the diagnosis finishes, so it includes the likely cause, as you chose;
  - **recovering** and **resolved:** sent for any watched incident, including ones where you approved "Add to watchlist", also as you chose.

**How I checked it**
- **Tests:** a fake Discord records every message. Three state changes produced exactly 3 messages, and repeating a state sent nothing more. Error responses from the webhook are retried, but a deleted webhook isn't.
- **In the browser** (my own server on 5181): a watched failing site showed as "New" with "1 new" 40 s after the cron check, and raised exactly one notification. Opening it cleared both.

**Not checked locally:** saving a webhook and "Send test" in the browser. Those need a local encryption key in `.dev.vars`; adding it was blocked because the safety check couldn't clear writing a secret, so I left it. The tests cover that flow. The old server on port 5173 is still broken.

**To do before webhooks work on the live site:** after merging `dev` into `main`, run this once from the repo. It makes a random key and stores it as a Worker secret, so the key never appears anywhere:

```
openssl rand -base64 32 | npx wrangler secret put WEBHOOK_KEY
```

Then save your Discord webhook in the sidebar, press "Send test", and run the demo watch walkthrough. You should get 3 messages: opened (with the cause), recovering, and resolved.

The next merge also applies a small database change (it records which address the webhook was saved from, so messages can link back to the incident). The deploy workflow applies it automatically.

### 🤖 Claude · 00:09:38

There's no fixed value to look up. The key is just 32 random bytes, written as a 44-character base64 string, and anyone setting up the app makes their own. A shared or published key would let anyone who has it read the saved webhook URLs, so it never goes in the repo.

**The example file:** I added `WEBHOOK_KEY` to `.dev.vars.example` with an **empty value**, plus comments on how to make a key. It's committed on `dev` (`93783fa`) and pushed. Left empty, webhooks are simply off, so it's safe as committed. The file now ends:

```
# Encrypts saved Slack/Discord webhook URLs (AES-GCM-256, TDD §11): 32 random bytes, base64. Empty = webhooks off.
# Make your own local key, and never commit one: in your .dev.vars, put the output of `openssl rand -base64 32`.
# Production uses a different key: `openssl rand -base64 32 | npx wrangler secret put WEBHOOK_KEY`.
# Changing the key makes saved webhooks unreadable; save them again afterwards.
WEBHOOK_KEY=
```

**Your actual keys:** use two separate ones, and don't paste either into the chat.
- **Local**, to try webhooks with `pnpm dev`. This adds a fresh key to your `.dev.vars`, which git ignores:
  ```
  echo "WEBHOOK_KEY=$(openssl rand -base64 32)" >> .dev.vars
  ```
  Then restart the dev server.
- **Live site**, once, after the merge. This sends the key straight to Cloudflare, so it's never saved in a file:
  ```
  openssl rand -base64 32 | npx wrangler secret put WEBHOOK_KEY
  ```

If you ever change a key, webhooks saved under the old one can't be read any more, so you'd just save your webhook URL again.

One step is still left for webhooks to work on the live site: setting the key, once.

```

```

After that, save your Discord webhook in the sidebar and press "Send test".
